Five terms, one diagram, four non-overlaps. The topic's highest-value trap is defused by geometry.

### 1. The terms, each defined once and from a source

| Term | Definition | Source | Its defining feature |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Desert** | A **climatic zone** — the arid core, where precipitation is greatly exceeded by potential evapotranspiration | ⚠️ Basic owner §8.4; 🔁 derived at **L1** | It is a **place**, defined by a water balance |
| **Aridity** | The **permanent climatic condition** of that water-balance deficit | ⚠️ Basic owner §8.1 | A **state**, not an event |
| **Drought** | ✅ *"A temporary reduction in water or moisture availability significantly below the normal or expected amount for a specific period"* | ✅ Khullar, local scan PDF p.135, folio 237 | **Temporary**; a **departure from a local normal**; occurs in humid regions too |
| **Land degradation** | Reduction or loss of biological or economic productivity of land, in **any** climate | ⚠️ Basic owner §8.4 and the terminology control | A **process**, climatically unrestricted |
| **Desertification** | ⚠️ *"Land degradation in arid, semi-arid and dry sub-humid areas, driven by climatic variation and human activity"* | ⚠️ Basic owner §8.4 | Land degradation **restricted to drylands** |

✅ The book definition, for comparison, is narrower and reveals its era: Khullar, folio 212 — *"Desertification can be defined as spread of desert like conditions in arid or semi-arid areas due to man's influence or climatic change."* ⚠️ ❗ Note the two differences from the canonical formulation: Khullar's version uses **"spread of desert like conditions"**, which imports the spatial-advance image that the Basic owner corrects, and it **omits the dry sub-humid class**, which the canonical definition includes. ❗ Report both; do not merge them.

✅ **The drought separation, given its full weight because it is the one definition available first-party from a book.** Khullar, folio 237, adds the institutional formulation: the **High Powered Committee on Disaster Management** holds that *"any lack of water to satisfy the normal needs of agriculture, livestock, industry or human population may be termed as a drought."*

⚠️ **Two features of drought matter here and nowhere else in this file.** ❗ It is defined **relative to a local normal**, so a rainfall total that would be a severe drought in Kerala may be an ordinary year in Barmer. ❗ And it is an **event**, with a beginning and an end, where aridity has neither. 🔁 **Disaster management owns everything that follows** — declaration, assessment, relief, contingency planning, the response architecture. ❗ **None of it appears in this file.**

### 2. ❗ The decisive sentence, and why it is decisive

⚠️ The Basic owner's §8.4 carries the sentence that this entire lesson exists to install: ❗ **"A desert cannot 'desertify'; the land at risk is the productive dryland margin around it."**

⚠️ **Unpack the logic, because it is airtight and reproducible.** ❗ Desertification is defined as **loss of productivity**. ❗ A hyper-arid desert core has **almost no biological or economic productivity to lose**. ❗ Therefore the core **cannot** desertify in any measurable sense. ❗ The land that **can** lose productivity is the land that **has** productivity: the **semi-arid and dry sub-humid margin**, where rainfall is sufficient to support grazing and cultivation and where people consequently live and work.

❗ **Three consequences, each of which pays in an answer.**

1. ❗ **The policy-relevant zone is the margin, not the core.** 🔁 This is the Basic owner's own §9.3 evidence unit, which states that degradation processes *"all operate on the semi-arid fringe where population and cultivation are viable"* and that recognising this *"redirects an answer from imagery about 'spreading sand' to the actual land-management question."*
2. ❗ **"The desert is spreading" is a category error, not merely an exaggeration.** 🔁 **Lesson 13** established that advancing-desert imagery is misleading; here the reason is formal. What is measured is **a change of condition class within the dryland zone**, not a movement of the zone's boundary.
3. ❗ **Desertification can occur hundreds of kilometres from any desert.** The dryland class includes **dry sub-humid** areas; a degrading semi-arid tract in Karnataka or Maharashtra is a desertification case with no sand dune within a thousand kilometres. ⚠️ 🔁 **Lesson 14's Table 7.3 proves the geography**: Karnataka is **72.60 per cent semi-arid** and Maharashtra **61.17 per cent**, neither of them north-western.

### 3. The four pathways — what desertification is actually made of

⚠️ If desertification is loss of dryland productivity, then it has as many mechanisms as there are ways to lose productivity. ❗ Four are established in this file, and naming all four is what converts the owned answer from recall into analysis.

| Pathway | Mechanism | Built at | Indian evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⚠️ **Aeolian / wind erosion** | Deflation strips the fine fraction; saltation abrades and ejects; suspension exports nutrients permanently; dunes mobilise | 🔁 **L4, L5, L6, L7, L9** | ✅ Khullar's wind-erosion statements; ✅ Fig. 7.2's **desert erosion (aeolian)** class and its limit line |
| ⚠️ **Hydric / water erosion** | Intense rain on crusted, bare ground generates high runoff; sheet, rill and gully erosion deepen into ravines | 🔁 **L8** for the arid regime; 🔁 Topic 05 owns the general process | ✅ **Table 7.1**, ravines, led by Uttar Pradesh at **12.30 lakh hectares** |
| ⚠️ **Chemical — salinisation and alkalinisation** | A raised water table plus evaporation relocates stored salt to the root zone; sodicity disperses aggregates and seals the surface | 🔁 **L9, L12** | ✅ **Table 7.2**, led by Uttar Pradesh at **12.95 lakh hectares**; ✅ about **80 lakh hectares, 2.43 per cent of India** |
| ⚠️ **Vegetative — cover and biomass loss** | Grazing above regenerative capacity, fuelwood offtake, felling and cultivation of marginal land reduce cover, which in turn enables the other three | 🔁 **L3** | ✅ Khullar's causes: *"uncontrolled grazing, reckless felling of trees and growing population"* |

❗ **The relationship between the four is the examinable part, and it is not a list.** ⚠️ The **vegetative pathway is the enabler**: removing cover lowers two rungs of Lesson 3's ladder, and the aeolian and hydric pathways both intensify as a direct result. ⚠️ The **chemical pathway runs in the opposite direction** — it is typically a consequence of **adding** water rather than losing it, which is why it appears in canal commands and is the one pathway that development can create. ❗ A candidate who states that **the pathways interact, with vegetation loss as the common amplifier and salinisation as the development-induced exception**, has given the examiner an organising structure rather than four facts.

✅ **Khullar's seven ecological implications**, at folio 212, map onto the pathways and are worth carrying as the **consequence set**: *"(i) Drifting of sand and its accumulation on fertile agricultural land. (ii) Excessive soil erosion by wind and to some extent by water. (iii) Deposition of sand in rivers, lakes and other water bodies thereby decreasing their water containing capacity. (iv) Lowering of water table leading to acute water shortage. (v) Increase in area under wastelands. (vi) Decrease in agricultural production. (vii) Increase in frequency and intensity of droughts."*

❗ **Note item (ii) in that list carefully.** ⚠️ Khullar's own sequence puts **wind first and water second**, with water as *"to some extent"* — ❗ while his **own tables** give ravines and salinity larger state-resolved footprints than his wind statements can reliably support. ⚠️ That internal tension is a further instance of Lesson 14's audit and should be noted, not resolved.

### 4. ❌ Correction box — what the 97.84 million hectare figure does and does not measure

> ❗ **The figure, with everything it must carry.** 📰 ✅ **97.84 million hectares**, of **land degradation and desertification**, as a **mapped condition for 2018-19**, on the authority of the **Desertification and Land Degradation Atlas of India published by the Space Applications Centre, Indian Space Research Organisation, Ahmedabad**, as reported by the **Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change** in Press Information Bureau release **1946401 of 07 August 2023**.
>
> ⚠️ **What it does measure.** ❗ A **mapped area in condition classes**, with — per the same release — an **online portal built with the Centre** that visualises degraded area *"with the processes causing degradation"*. 📰 ✅ Per **CA-1**, the atlas *"provides state wise area of degraded lands for the time frame 2018-19"*. 📰 ✅ Per **CA-5**, it sits in a published **series** with a **Desertification Status Mapping Technical Guideline**. ❗ It is therefore **process-attributed, state-resolved, class-based and methodologically documented** — everything Lesson 14's textbook rows were not.
>
> ❗ **What it does not measure.** ⚠️ It is **not a desert area**. It is **not the Thar's area**. It is **not a wind-erosion figure** — wind is one of the processes within it. It is **not a 2026 measurement**; the Basic owner's date-and-data control says so explicitly: *"the SAC atlas reports 2018-19 mapped condition, not a 2026 measurement."* And it is **not a rate**.
>
> ❗ **The trend question, handled precisely.** 📰 ✅ CA-1 describes *"the change analysis for the duration of 15 years, from 2003-05 to 2018-19."* ⚠️ That is a **two-epoch comparison**, which supports a statement of **direction between two mapped periods** and nothing more. ❗ It **cannot** be converted into an annual rate; it **cannot** describe the path between the epochs; and ⚠️ it **cannot by itself distinguish** a change in land condition from a change in classification or sensor between assessments. ❗ **No annual rate appears anywhere in this file.**
>
> ⚠️ **The rounding, disposed of once.** 🔁 **Lesson 14** established it: the advanced owner's **97.85** and the Ministry's **97.84** are the **same assessment**, not two findings. ❗ **The first-party figure controls.** ❗ The owner's percentages — **~29.7 per cent** for 2018-19 and **28.76 per cent** for 2003-05 — are retained **only** with the explicit note that they were **not obtained first-party this pass**, and that the **17 June 2021 release states no national figure at all**.

### 5. ❌ Correction box — the advancing desert, restated at the definitional level

> ⚠️ 🔁 **Lesson 13** reported four statements — a textbook rate of about half a kilometre a year, the canonical correction that advancing-desert imagery is misleading, the Aravalli Green Wall's stated objective of preventing eastward expansion of the Thar, and the Supreme Court's description of the range as a barrier against desertification. ❗ Here the correction is made at the level of **what the word means**, which is stronger than arguing about a number.
>
> ❗ **The definitional argument.** Desertification is **land degradation occurring in drylands**. **Land degradation is a loss of productivity of a piece of land.** ⚠️ A loss of productivity is a property **of a place**, measured by comparing that place with its own earlier condition. ❗ **It therefore has no direction of travel.** A hectare in Barmer that has lost cover has not moved; it has changed.
>
> ⚠️ **What the imagery gets right, and this must be conceded.** ❗ **Sand does move.** 🔁 Lesson 6 established dune mobility; ✅ Khullar's own mechanism is explicit: *"The sand carried by wind is deposited on the adjoining fertile lands whose fertility dwindles and sometimes the fertile land merges with the advancing desert."* ❗ **Local encroachment of mobile sand onto cultivated land is a real, observable process**, and it is one of the aeolian pathway's effects. ⚠️ What does not follow is that **the desert as a climatic zone** is marching eastward at a measurable rate.
>
> ❗ **The precise formulation, which concedes what is true and refuses what is not.** *"Mobile sand can and does encroach locally onto cultivated land at a desert margin, and that is one aeolian pathway of degradation. But desertification is defined as loss of dryland productivity, which is a change in a place rather than a movement of a boundary, and India's own national assessment accordingly reports degraded area by condition class, state and process for a mapped period rather than tracking a frontier."*
>
> ❗ **And the hand-off is visible.** 🔁 **The question of what the convention, the state or the court should do about desertification belongs to the environment-and-ecology owner.** This file stops at *"what physical process is occurring and how would we measure it?"*

### 6. The international frame, named with dates and routed

📰 ✅ **CA-6 — the United Nations Convention to Combat Desertification.** Its sixteenth session of the Conference of the Parties, **COP16, took place in Riyadh, Saudi Arabia, from 2 to 13 December 2024**, under the theme ***"Our Land. Our Future."*** ✅ The Convention is *"the global voice for land and one of the three major UN treaties known as the Rio Conventions, alongside climate and biodiversity."* ✅ The Conference of the Parties is *"the main decision-making body of UNCCD's 197 Parties — 196 countries and the European Union."* ✅ COP16 coincided with the **thirtieth anniversary** of the Convention, was *"the largest UN land conference to date"* and was *"the first UNCCD COP held in the Middle East and North Africa region."*

📰 ✅ **India's position, from CA-1.** India **hosted COP14 in September 2019**, and *"is striving towards achieving the national commitments of Land Degradation Neutrality (LDN) and restoration of 26 Million ha of degraded land by 2030."*

❗ **Three disciplines apply, and all three are enforced here.**

- ❗ **Commitment is not outcome.** ⚠️ **26 million hectares by 2030 is a pledge.** It is not measured restoration, and 🔁 **Lesson 16** shows why the distinction matters operationally. ❗ The terminology control says it plainly: *"restoration commitment differs from measured restoration outcome."*
- ❗ **The three instruments are never summed, subtracted or compared.** ⚠️ 🔁 Lesson 16 sets them out; here it is enough to say that **Land Degradation Neutrality under this Convention**, the **Bonn Challenge pledge**, and the **afforestation reported under the Twenty Point Programme** are **three distinct instruments with three different baselines and three different reporting bodies**, and that **no fetched document reconciles them**. ❗ **They do not appear as one number anywhere in this file.**
- 🔁 **The Convention's architecture is routed.** ❗ Its organs, its reporting cycle, its financial mechanism, the content of its decisions and the Land Degradation Neutrality target-setting methodology belong to the **environment-and-ecology owner**. ❗ **This file names the Convention, dates its most recent Conference and stops.**

### 7. 🔁 The 2020 demand — a bounded cross-owner application, and then the hand-off

⚠️ **A1 — 2020, General Studies Paper I, Question 5:** *"Desertification as a process without climatic boundaries"*, 10 marks, 150 words, to be justified with examples. ❗ The routing ledger assigns it to **Environment and Ecology, `basic/23_Desertification-UNCCD-and-Land-Degradation.md`**. ❗ **It is not a Topic-07 answer target, and no model answer to it appears in this file.**

⚠️ **Why it is used here at all.** ❗ Because it is **the single best available demonstration that the desert-against-desertification separation is examinable**, and because this lesson's nested-set diagram is exactly what the demand's phrasing turns on. ⚠️ The phrase *"without climatic boundaries"* is testing whether a candidate knows that **desertification is not confined to deserts** — which is non-overlap [3] in the diagram: degradation restricted to the **dryland class**, which includes the **dry sub-humid** belt, not restricted to the **arid core**.

❗ **What this file contributes, and the boundary at which it stops.** ⚠️ **Contribution:** the physical separation of the five terms; the four pathways; the demonstration from Table 7.3 that semi-arid India includes Karnataka at 72.60 per cent and Maharashtra at 61.17 per cent, so the geography of the at-risk class is national rather than north-western; and the reason the margin rather than the core is at risk. ❗ **Hand-off:** the moment the question becomes *"what should the convention, the state or the court do about it?"*, 🔁 **the answer belongs to the environment-and-ecology owner**, by this topic's own boundary clause.

### 8. 🇮🇳 India example and its limitation — the dryland class, mapped

⚠️ The Indian case for this lesson **is the national assessment**, and its limitations are the most important in the file.

❗ **Limitation one: no process breakdown is available here.** ⚠️ The portal exists and attributes processes; ❗ **the figures behind it live in a publication this edition did not obtain.** ❗ **No aeolian share of India's 97.84 million hectares is stated anywhere in this file**, and Lesson 18 treats that absence as a stated limit on the owned answer's precision.
❗ **Limitation two: no state-wise figure was obtained first-party.** ⚠️ The advanced owner's **ranking** — Rajasthan carries the largest desertified area — and its **aeolian-share statement for Rajasthan** are used with the owner named and the period given. ❗ **No percentage is attached to Rajasthan's total.**
❗ **Limitation three: reversibility has no timescale.** ⚠️ The Basic owner holds that dryland degradation is *"often recoverable"*; 🔁 **Lesson 9** recorded that **no source available here gives a recovery timescale** for a broken pavement, a destabilised dune field or a salinised command. ❗ **"Often reversible" is a statement about possibility, not about speed or cost**, and this file does not inflate it into one.
❗ **Limitation four: the Centre's index page is undated**, so it is cited as fetched during this edition's preparation and the **2021** edition is named explicitly rather than called "the latest".

### Comparison, objection and the residual

| | Desert | Desertification |
|---|---|---|
| Kind of thing | A **place** — a climatic zone | A **process** — loss of productivity |
| Defined by | A water-balance deficit | A **change** in land capability |
| Where it is | The arid core | ❗ The **productive dryland margin**, including dry sub-humid areas |
| Can it be reversed? | ❗ **No** — the climate is not reversible by land management | ⚠️ **Often**, per the canonical owner; ❗ no timescale available |
| How measured | Climatic classification | ✅ **Mapped condition classes by state and process** |
| Does it move? | ⚠️ Its boundary shifts with climate over long periods | ❗ **No** — it is a change in place, not a frontier |
| Who owns the policy? | 🔁 Climate owners | 🔁 **Environment and Ecology** |

**Strongest objection.** This is definitional pedantry. An examiner marking a 150-word answer will not award marks for distinguishing five words.

**Reply.** The examiner will award marks for an answer that **does not make the category error**, and will deduct for one that does. ❗ More concretely, the owned 2026 demand reads *"the role of aeolian processes in desertification and land degradation"* — ⚠️ **the question itself uses both terms, deliberately and separately**. ❗ A candidate who treats them as synonyms has failed to notice that the examiner distinguished them in the question. ⚠️ And the practical payoff is immediate: knowing that desertification is **degradation restricted to drylands** tells a candidate that **the aeolian pathway is one of four**, that **the at-risk zone is the margin**, and that **Karnataka and Maharashtra belong in the answer's geography** — three analytical moves that a synonym-user cannot make.

⚠️ **Residual.** ❗ The **boundary of the dryland class is itself a convention.** Where the dry sub-humid class ends and the humid class begins depends on the aridity index threshold adopted, and **different conventions move the line**, changing how much of any country counts as at risk. ❗ **No threshold value is stated anywhere in this file**, and the extent of the dryland class is therefore reported as **what a named assessment mapped**, never as a natural fact.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** *"Geographical features and their location-changes… and the effects of such changes"*, General Studies Paper I. 🔁 The convention, its instruments and the policy demand are routed to the environment-and-ecology owner; drought response is routed to disaster management.
- **Verified linkage:** ✅ **2026 General Studies Paper I, Question 6** — this lesson supplies the **limiting argument** element in its definitional form and bounds every other element. 🔁 **A1 — 2020 General Studies Paper I, Question 5** — named, used as a bounded application, **owned by the environment-and-ecology file**, no model answer given here. 🔁 **A4 — 2018 Prelims, Question 82** — named at Lesson 14; owned elsewhere; ❗ **no key recorded or inferred.**
- **Traps:** using desert and desertification interchangeably; saying a desert is desertifying; confining desertification to the north-west; equating desertification with sand advance; treating the national figure as a desert area or a trend; converting the two-epoch comparison into a rate; confusing drought with aridity; treating a restoration pledge as a measured outcome.
- **Answer use:** this is the sentence that should appear in every answer on the Indian dryland. *"Desertification is land degradation occurring in arid, semi-arid and dry sub-humid areas; a desert cannot desertify, because the land at risk is the productive margin, and the process operates through four pathways — wind erosion, water erosion, salinisation and vegetation loss — of which the aeolian is one."* ❗ Twelve seconds of writing, and it bounds the entire answer correctly.

Revision notes:

- ❗ **The five terms:** **desert** = a climatic **zone** · **aridity** = a permanent **state** · **drought** = a temporary **event** · **land degradation** = productivity loss in **any** climate · **desertification** = land degradation **restricted to drylands**.
- ⚠️ **Canonical definition:** *"Desertification is land degradation in arid, semi-arid and dry sub-humid areas, driven by climatic variation and human activity."* ⚠️ **Book definition (Khullar, folio 212):** *"spread of desert like conditions in arid or semi-arid areas due to man's influence or climatic change."* ❗ The book version **imports the spread image** and **omits dry sub-humid**. Report both.
- ✅ **Drought (Khullar, folio 237):** *"a temporary reduction in water or moisture availability significantly below the normal or expected amount for a specific period."* ✅ **High Powered Committee on Disaster Management:** *"Any lack of water to satisfy the normal needs of agriculture, livestock, industry or human population may be termed as a drought."* 🔁 Response doctrine routed.
- ❗ **The decisive sentence:** *"A desert cannot 'desertify'; the land at risk is the productive dryland margin around it."* ❗ Because degradation is **loss of productivity**, and the core has none to lose.
- ❗ **Four non-overlaps:** desert ≠ desertification (zone vs process) · aridity ≠ drought (state vs event) · degradation ≠ desertification (the latter is climate-restricted) · desertification ≠ sand advance (capability loss **in place**).
- ⚠️ **Four pathways:** **aeolian** (L4-L7, L9) · **hydric** (L8; Table 7.1 ravines, UP 12.30) · **chemical / salinisation** (L9, L12; Table 7.2, UP 12.95; ~80 lakh ha ≈ 2.43 % of India) · **vegetative** (L3; grazing, felling, population). ❗ **Vegetation loss is the common amplifier; salinisation is the development-induced exception.**
- ✅ **Khullar's seven implications:** sand drift onto fertile land · excessive soil erosion by wind and *"to some extent"* water · sand deposition in water bodies · lowering of the water table · more wasteland · less agricultural production · more frequent and intense droughts. ❗ Note his *"to some extent"* on water is in tension with **his own tables**.
- ❗ **[N9] 97.84 Mha, with everything it must carry:** land degradation **and** desertification · **mapped condition for 2018-19** · **SAC-ISRO atlas** · reported by the Ministry, **PIB 1946401, 07 Aug 2023**. ❗ **Not** a desert area, **not** a Thar figure, **not** wind-only, **not** 2026, **not** a rate. ❗ 97.85 and 97.84 are **one finding**, not two.
- ❗ **Two-epoch comparison (2003-05 → 2018-19), per CA-1:** direction only; ❗ **no annual rate**; ⚠️ cannot by itself separate real change from method change. ❗ The **17 Jun 2021** release states **no national figure**.
- ❗ **[N1] at the definitional level:** sand **does** encroach locally — ✅ Khullar: *"sometimes the fertile land merges with the advancing desert"* — but the **zone** does not march. ❗ Concede the local process; refuse the frontier claim.
- 📰 ✅ **CA-6, UNCCD:** **COP16, Riyadh, 2-13 December 2024**; theme ***"Our Land. Our Future."***; one of the three **Rio Conventions**; **197 Parties — 196 countries and the European Union**; **30th anniversary**; largest UN land conference to date; first UNCCD COP in the Middle East and North Africa region · 📰 ✅ **CA-1, India:** hosted **COP14 in September 2019**; striving toward **Land Degradation Neutrality and restoration of 26 million ha by 2030**. ❗ **A pledge, not an outcome.**
- ❗ **Three instruments, never summed:** LDN under the Convention · the **Bonn Challenge** pledge · **Twenty Point Programme** afforestation. ❗ No fetched document reconciles them.
- 🔁 **A1 (2020 GS-I Q5)**, *"desertification as a process without climatic boundaries"* — **owned by the environment-and-ecology file**. ⚠️ Used as a **bounded application**; ❗ **no model answer to it appears here**; the hand-off is at *"what should the convention or the state do?"*
- ❗ **Limitations:** **no aeolian share** of India's degraded area stated · **no state-wise figure** obtained first-party · **no recovery timescale** for reversibility · the Centre's index page is **undated**, and the **2021** edition is named explicitly.
- ❗ **The dryland boundary is a convention.** ❗ No aridity-index threshold is stated anywhere in this file.

### Concept check

**Question:** A district in Karnataka reports falling crop yields, thinning tree cover, gullying on sloping fields and rising salinity in an irrigated block. An official writes: "This cannot be desertification — there is no desert within a thousand kilometres, and no sand is advancing." Assess the reasoning, and state what the district's situation actually is.

**Model answer:** The reasoning fails twice, once on the **definition** and once on the **geography**. On the definition, desertification is **land degradation occurring in arid, semi-arid and dry sub-humid areas**; it is a **process of productivity loss**, not a property of proximity to sand. The canonical owner's view is precisely that a desert cannot desertify, because the arid core has almost no productivity left to lose, and that the land at risk is the **productive dryland margin**. Requiring a nearby desert inverts the definition. On the geography, Karnataka is overwhelmingly within the dryland class: Khullar's Table 7.3 records it as **72.60 per cent semi-arid**, so the great majority of the state falls inside the zone in which degradation counts as desertification. What the district is experiencing is desertification through **three of the four pathways simultaneously**. Thinning tree cover is the **vegetative** pathway, and it is the amplifier, since loss of cover exposes the surface to both wind and raindrop impact. Gullying on sloping fields is the **hydric** pathway, the same process that Khullar's Table 7.1 tabulates as ravines across nine states, led by Uttar Pradesh rather than Rajasthan. Rising salinity in the irrigated block is the **chemical** pathway, and it is the development-induced exception: irrigation without adequate drainage raises the water table, and evaporation relocates salt that was already stored in the profile up into the root zone. Only the aeolian pathway is likely to be minor here. The official's error is the single highest-value trap in this topic, and the correct framing is that **desertification is defined by climate class and productivity change, not by the presence of sand**.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not use the presence or absence of dunes as a test for desertification. Sand is one expression of one of four pathways; the test is whether land within the arid, semi-arid or dry sub-humid class is losing biological or economic productivity.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "A desert cannot desertify." Explain this statement and assess what it implies for where degradation should be measured. *(Explain and assess — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (about 150 words):** Desertification is land degradation occurring in arid, semi-arid and dry sub-humid areas, and land degradation means loss of biological or economic productivity. A hyper-arid core has almost no productivity to lose, so it cannot register the change the definition requires. The land that can lose productivity is the land that has it — the semi-arid and dry sub-humid margin, where rainfall supports grazing and cultivation and where people therefore live and work. Three implications follow. Measurement should be concentrated on the margin rather than the core, which is what a class-based national assessment does when it reports degraded area by state and by causal process. The geography of concern is national rather than north-western: Khullar's Table 7.3 records Karnataka at 72.60 per cent semi-arid and Maharashtra at 61.17. And the imagery of a spreading desert is a category error, since what is measured is capability loss in place.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 2 for the definition of desertification stated with its climate-class restriction; 2 for the productivity argument deriving why the core cannot desertify; 4 for the three implications — measure at the margin, a national rather than north-western geography with sourced evidence, and the category-error correction on spreading; 1 for the class-based national assessment identified as the appropriate instrument; 1 for a sourced state figure used as evidence. Deduct 2 for treating desert and desertification as interchangeable.

## Lesson 16 — Restoration: matching the measure to the process

Advanced owner §2's conservation-direction row — *"agroforestry, pasture management, water conservation and reclamation"* — and §6's current links.

Khullar, local scan PDF p.123, folio 212, for the five measures of controlling desertification including the **Central Arid Zone Research Institute** mulching recommendation and the shifting-dune districts with their area; folio 213 for the soil-conservation framing, the named authorities and the forest-cover norm; local scan PDF p.122, folio 211, for the **Indira Gandhi Canal trade-off**. Boundary: 🔁 **The agriculture and soils owners own conservation agronomy** — crop rotation, strip cropping, contour ploughing, terracing and agroforestry practice. ❗ This lesson **names the ladder and hands off**; it teaches no practice. 🔁 **The water-resources and economy owners** own canal command design and irrigation engineering; this lesson teaches the **Indira Gandhi Canal trade-off as a land-degradation outcome** and stops before canal management. 🔁 **The environment-and-ecology owner** owns the convention, the instruments and the policy demand. ❗ **No Great Indian Bustard content and no Desert National Park management anywhere in this file.**
Trap: the undifferentiated measure list. Writing *"afforestation, shelterbelts, water harvesting and grazing control"* with no pathway attached scores almost nothing, because it could be written without knowing anything about the place.

   THE READING: four pathways, and almost no measure treats more than
   two of them. The canal row is the decisive one - a development
   intervention that is STRONGLY POSITIVE in three columns and

One grid, one argument: a measure is not good or bad in itself; it is matched or mismatched to a pathway at a place.

### 1. The principle, stated before the measures

⚠️ 🔁 **Lesson 15** established that desertification runs through **four pathways** — aeolian, hydric, chemical and vegetative. ❗ It follows immediately that a restoration measure can only be assessed **against a pathway**, and that the same measure can be excellent in one column and useless or harmful in another.

❗ **Three corollaries, each of which is an answer-writing instruction.**

1. ❗ **Never present measures as a list.** ⚠️ A list is pathway-blind and could be written about anywhere. **Present them as matched pairs**: this pathway, therefore this measure.
2. ❗ **Always state the place.** 🔁 **Lesson 11's** mosaic means the dominant pathway differs between the western Marusthali, the Bagar and the canal command. A measure correct at Barmer may be beside the point near Sikar.
3. ❗ **Always separate the physical measure from the instrument that funds or pledges it.** ⚠️ A **shelterbelt** is a physical intervention with a mechanism; a **26-million-hectare pledge** is a commitment; an **18.94-million-hectare reported afforestation area** is programme reporting under a different scheme. ❗ The terminology control insists on it: *"restoration commitment differs from measured restoration outcome."*

### 2. The book's five measures, each with its mechanism supplied

✅ Khullar's folio 212 lists five *"Measures of Controlling Desertification"*. ⚠️ The source gives the measures; **this edition supplies the mechanism for each**, drawing on Arc A, because a measure without a mechanism cannot be matched to a pathway.

| ✅ Khullar's measure | ⚠️ Its mechanism, from Arc A | Pathway treated |
|---|---|---|
| ✅ *"Intensive tree plantation programme should be initiated."* | 🔁 **L3, rungs two and three**: a canopy absorbs wind momentum before it reaches the ground, roots bind the substrate and litter covers it; cover also intercepts raindrop impact | **Aeolian** and **vegetative**, with hydric benefit |
| ✅ **Mulching in small squares**, with the mechanism in the source's own words | 🔁 **L4**: a physical barrier interrupts the **saltation cascade** at the surface. Breaking the chain of impacts stops the self-amplification before it builds | **Aeolian**, directly |
| ✅ *"Grazing should be controlled and new pastures should be developed."* | 🔁 **L9**: concentrated grazing **breaks the desert-pavement lag** and shatters the soil crust; 🔁 **L3**: hooves remove and prevent cover. **New pasture** adds supply so that existing pasture is not grazed beyond recovery | **Vegetative** — the enabler — and therefore **aeolian** and **hydric** indirectly |
| ✅ *"Indiscriminate felling of trees should be banned."* | 🔁 **L3**: removing woody cover lowers rung two over a wide area at once | **Vegetative**, principally |
| ✅ *"Alternative sources of fuel can reduce the demand for fuelwood and save the trees from destruction hence checking the onward march of the desert."* | ⚠️ A **demand-side** measure: it removes the driver rather than treating the symptom | **Vegetative**, upstream |

✅ **The mulching entry, quoted in full because it is the most specific technical recommendation in the Indian material:** *"Shifting sand dunes in Bikaner, Barmer, Churu, Jaisalmer and Jhunjhunun districts of Rajasthan cover an approximate area of 74 thousand sq km. Central Arid Zone Research Institute, Jodhpur has suggested mulching them with different plant species. Mulches are put in small squares and serve as an effective physical barrier to the moving sand."*

❗ **Three things in that passage are worth isolating.** ⚠️ The **five districts** — **Bikaner, Barmer, Churu, Jaisalmer and Jhunjhunun** — are the named shifting-dune tract, and ❗ they sit on the **western, arid side** of Lesson 11's transect, which is exactly where Lesson 6 predicts mobile dunes. ⚠️ The **approximate area of 74 thousand square kilometres** is a source-era book figure with no stated agency, method or date, and is quoted as such. ❗ And the mechanism — *"an effective physical barrier to the moving sand"* — is **a saltation-interruption measure**, which is why the grid places it at the top of the aeolian column and nowhere else.

❗ **A disclosure attaches to the Institute.** ⚠️ Its own website **could not be reached** during the preparation of this edition. ❗ The Institute is therefore **named on the authority of Khullar's folios 76 to 77 and 212, and no publication of its own is quoted anywhere in this file.**

### 3. ❌ Correction box — the canal trade-off, and why it is the lesson's centre

> ✅ **The statement, from Khullar at folio 211:** *"Although Indira Gandhi canal in Rajasthan has turned the sandy desert into a granary, it has given birth to serious problems of salinity and alkalinity."*
>
> ✅ **The canal's recorded scale:** Majid Husain's India extremes list at printed p.10.5 gives *"Longest Canal: Indira Gandhi Canal, 682 km in length."* ❗ A source-era book figure, quoted with its page and its era.
>
> ⚠️ **Why this is the single most instructive case in the lesson.** ❗ Irrigation is, in grid terms, a **three-column success and a one-column failure**. ⚠️ It **adds water to a water-limited system**, so biomass rises; rising biomass **fixes dunes**, which treats the aeolian pathway; and cultivated, covered ground treats the vegetative pathway. ❗ But it **raises the water table**, and 🔁 **Lessons 9 and 12** supply the consequence: the salt is **already in the profile and the shallow groundwater**, accumulated over a long arid history and in closed basins, and a raised water table plus high evaporative demand **brings it up** by capillary rise into the root zone and onto the surface.
>
> ❗ **The crucial point: irrigation does not import the salt. It relocates salt that was already there.** ⚠️ That is why the problem is **specific to arid commands** and why it is largely **avoidable with drainage** rather than inherent to irrigation.
>
> ⚠️ **The grid's own conclusion.** ❗ The chemical column has **one strongly matched measure** — **drainage with leaching** — and **the canal row is the only row marked counter-productive**. ❗ A programme that stabilises dunes by irrigating without providing drainage has **traded one degradation pathway for another**, and an answer that says so has made the sharpest available point about Indian dryland policy.
>
> 🔁 **The hand-off is immediate and visible.** ❗ Canal command design, conveyance efficiency, lining, drainage engineering, water allocation and cropping-pattern regulation **belong to the water-resources and agriculture owners**. ❗ **This file states the land-degradation outcome and stops.**

### 4. The policy and programme layer, dated and bounded

📰 ✅ **The Aravalli Green Wall Project — CA-3, 25 March 2023.** The project greens a **5 kilometre buffer area around the Aravalli Hill Range** across **Haryana, Rajasthan, Gujarat and Delhi**, *"where the Aravalli hills landscape span over 6 million hectares of land"*. ✅ Its method is *"planting native species of trees and shrubs on scrubland, wasteland and degraded forest land, along with rejuvenating and restoring surface water bodies such as ponds, lakes and streams"*, plus *"agroforestry and pasture development to enhance the livelihoods of local communities"*. ✅ Its stated objectives include improving *"the ecological health of the Aravalli range"* and *"To prevent eastward expansion of Thar Desert and to reduce land degradation by creating green barriers that will prevent soil erosion, desertification and dust storms."* ✅ On the same day the Ministry unveiled the **National Action Plan to Combat Desertification and Land Degradation Through Forestry Interventions** and an **agroforestry FAQ published by the Indian Council of Forestry Research and Education**.

⚠️ **Read the project through the grid and it scores well in three columns.** ❗ Native species on **scrubland, wasteland and degraded forest land** is **cover restoration** — the vegetative column and, through it, the aeolian one. ❗ **Rejuvenating ponds, lakes and streams** is the **hydric and recharge** column. ❗ **Agroforestry and pasture development** addresses the **driver**, by giving the grazing and fuelwood demand somewhere else to go. ⚠️ ❗ **The chemical column is not addressed, and the project does not claim to address it.**

❗ **[N11] The corridor-length figure is excluded.** ⚠️ A description of the Green Wall as **"1,400 kilometres long and 5 kilometres wide"** circulates widely. ❗ **It does not appear in the first-party release**, which gives only the **5 kilometre buffer**, the **four states** and the **approximately 6 million hectare landscape**. ❗ **No corridor length is stated anywhere in this file.** ⚠️ If one is required, the honest answer is that **the first-party release does not give one**.

❗ **No outcome is claimed.** ⚠️ Neither release reports measured restoration, measured dune stabilisation or a measured change in degradation class attributable to any programme. ❗ **A launch is not an outcome.**

### 5. ❌ Correction box — three instruments, three baselines, never one number

> ⚠️ Three restoration commitments circulate together and are constantly conflated. ❗ They are **three distinct instruments** and this edition **never sums, subtracts or compares them**.
>
> | Instrument | What it is | Figure, as the first-party source states it | Baseline and reporting |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | 📰 ✅ **Bonn Challenge pledge** | A **voluntary restoration pledge** made under a climate process | At the climate Conference in **Paris, 2015**, India pledged *"13 million hectares (mha) of degraded and deforested land by the year 2020, and additional 8 mha by 2030"* — *"to restore of 21 m.ha by 2030"* — *"increased to **26 mha**, during the COP14 to the United Nations Convention to Combat Desertification in 2019"* | ✅ **IUCN-India** *"has been assigned for reporting India's progress on achieving Bonn Challenge target"* |
> | 📰 ✅ **Land Degradation Neutrality** | A **target concept** under the desertification Convention | ✅ India *"striving towards achieving the national commitments of Land Degradation Neutrality (LDN) and restoration of 26 Million ha of degraded land by 2030"* | 🔁 The Convention's own reporting cycle — **owned by the environment-and-ecology file** |
> | 📰 ✅ **Twenty Point Programme afforestation** | **Programme reporting** of area covered by afforestation | *"approximately **18.94 million hectares** during the period **2011-12 to 2021-22**"* | A domestic programme-reporting series, covering *"afforestation achievements under concerted efforts of the State Governments through various central and state specific schemes"* |
>
> ❗ **Why they must not be combined.** ⚠️ The first two are **pledges about the future**; the third is **reported area under a different activity over a stated past decade**. ⚠️ **Afforestation area is not the same thing as restoration of degraded land**, and neither is the same thing as a measured reduction in degraded area. ❗ **No fetched document reconciles the three**, and this edition records that non-reconciliation as an **open point** rather than closing it with arithmetic.
>
> ⚠️ **The related national forest goal, named once.** 📰 ✅ CA-2 records the **National Forest Policy, 1988** goal of *"a minimum of one-third of the total land area under forest or tree cover"*. ✅ Khullar's folio 213 gives the historical antecedent: a forest-cover norm *"considered healthy for soil and water conservation"* of **20 to 25 per cent**, *"raised to 33 per cent in the second five year plan; the proportion being 20 per cent for the plains and 60 per cent for hilly and mountainous regions."* ⚠️ ❗ **A cover norm is a forestry target, not a degradation measurement**, and it is named here only because it is the policy frame in which the afforestation figure sits.
>
> 📰 ✅ **Two further institutional facts, named and not developed:** a **Centre of Excellence envisaged at the Indian Council of Forestry Research and Education, Dehradun**, *"for enhanced South-South Cooperation"*; and an **online portal developed with the Space Applications Centre** for visualising degraded area with its causal processes. 🔁 Institutional architecture is routed.

### 6. The conservation-practice ladder — named, and handed off

✅ Khullar's folios 213 to 215 move from desertification into **soil conservation** generally, and the material there is **cross-owner**. ⚠️ The ladder is named here so that a candidate knows where it sits, and nothing more is taught.

- ✅ **Afforestation**, with the forest-cover norms above.
- ✅ **Checking overgrazing**, with grazing grounds *"properly checked"* and *"separate grazing grounds should be earmarked and fodder crops should be grown in larger quantities"*.
- ✅ **Constructing dams**, to check *"the speed of water"* and save soil from erosion.
- ✅ **Changing agricultural practices** — 🔁 **crop rotation, strip cropping, contour ploughing and terracing**, which Khullar treats at folios 214 to 215 with figures. ❗ **This is conservation agronomy and belongs to the agriculture and soils owners. It is named here and not taught.**

✅ **The historical-policy framing, worth one line for its rhetorical value in an answer.** Khullar cites **S.P. Chatterjee** — *"soil erosion is the greatest single evil to Indian agriculture and animal husbandry"* — and **S.I. Kayastha** — *"with soil conservation people rise and with its destruction they fall"*; and attributes to **G.T. Renner** the view that conservation *"is defined as 'the greatest good to the largest number for the longest time'"*. ✅ He records that *"about two thirds of our arable land needs conservation measures."* ⚠️ These are **mid-century authorities quoted by a textbook**, usable as **attributed framing**, not as current assessment.

### 7. 🇮🇳 India example and its limitation — matching, in practice, along the transect

⚠️ The grid becomes concrete when it is laid along 🔁 **Lesson 11's** west-to-east transect.

| Subregion | Dominant pathway | Matched measures | What would be mismatched |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Western Marusthali** — Bikaner, Barmer, Churu, Jaisalmer, Jhunjhunun; shifting dunes; mean dune heights to about 30 m at Barmer | ⚠️ **Aeolian** | ✅ **CAZRI mulch squares** as a physical barrier to moving sand; shelterbelts; plantation with protection | ❗ Drainage works, where there is no salinity problem to drain |
| **Eastern Marusthali** — rocky, bedrock outcrops, pavement surfaces | ⚠️ **Aeolian, but armour-dependent** | ⚠️ **Protecting the lag** — grazing routing away from concentration points, control of vehicle tracks and quarry access | ❗ Dune stabilisation, where there is little free sand |
| **Rajasthan Bagar** — semi-arid, steppe vegetation, cultivated **rohi** patches, seasonal Aravalli streams | ⚠️ **Vegetative and hydric** | ⚠️ Cover management; **controlled grazing with new pastures**; **water harvesting and stream rejuvenation**; agroforestry | ❗ Sand barriers, which address a pathway that is not dominant here |
| **Canal command** | ❗ **Chemical** | ❗ **Drainage with leaching** — the only strongly matched measure in that column | ❗ More plantation alone, which does not lower a water table and may raise local evapotranspiration without treating the salt store |
| **Aravalli buffer** — scrubland, wasteland, degraded forest, across four states | ⚠️ **Vegetative, with hydric recharge** | 📰 ✅ The **Green Wall's own method**: native species, water-body rejuvenation, agroforestry and pasture | ❗ Reading it as a barrier against a moving frontier (🔁 **L13**, **N13**) |

❗ **The limitations, five of them.**

- ❗ **No measured outcome exists in any source here** for any Indian restoration programme. ⚠️ Launch dates, buffer widths, landscape extents and pledges are available; **measured change in degradation class attributable to a programme is not**.
- ❗ **No cost, no benefit-cost ratio and no survival rate** for any plantation or stabilisation programme appears in any source available to this edition.
- ❗ **No recovery timescale.** 🔁 **Lessons 9 and 15** recorded it: *"often reversible"* is a statement about **possibility**, not about **speed**, and no source here supplies a duration for pavement re-formation, dune fixation or salinity reclamation.
- ❗ **The Institute's own publications are not quoted**, because its site could not be reached; it is named on the textbook's authority only.
- ❗ **The 74-thousand-square-kilometre dune-tract figure** carries **no agency, method or date** and is quoted strictly as a source-era book figure with its folio.

### Comparison, objection and the residual

**The comparison that organises the whole lesson is physical measure against policy instrument.**

| | Physical measure | Policy instrument |
|---|---|---|
| Example | Mulch squares; shelterbelt; drainage; controlled grazing | Bonn Challenge pledge; Land Degradation Neutrality target; a national action plan |
| What it acts on | ⚠️ **A pathway at a place** | ⚠️ **An aggregate, nationally or globally** |
| How it is assessed | Does it interrupt the mechanism? | Is the committed quantity delivered and reported? |
| Failure mode | ❗ **Mismatch** — right measure, wrong pathway | ❗ **Commitment-outcome gap** — pledged, not measured |
| Who owns it here | ⚠️ **This topic**, insofar as it is matched to a physical process | 🔁 **Environment and Ecology** |

**Strongest objection.** Restoration is policy, and the boundary clause routes policy to another owner. This lesson is trespassing.

**Reply.** The boundary is drawn by the **agency test**, not by the subject matter. ❗ *"What physical process is occurring and how would we measure it?"* is this topic's; *"what should the state or the convention do?"* is not. ⚠️ **Matching a measure to a pathway is a physical question**: it asks whether a given intervention interrupts a given mechanism, and the answer comes from Arc A, not from policy analysis. ❗ The Basic owner's ownership clause names **canal trade-offs, land-degradation neutrality and process-matched restoration** inside the owned core precisely on that basis. ⚠️ What is **not** taken here is extensive and is visible throughout: no convention architecture, no financing, no implementation framework, no scheme evaluation, no conservation agronomy, no canal engineering, no protected-area management and no species conservation of any kind.

⚠️ **Residual.** ❗ **Whether India's restoration programmes are achieving measured reductions in degraded area is not answerable from this evidence base.** ⚠️ The only national measurement available is the **two-epoch comparison**, which 🔁 **Lesson 15** showed cannot be converted into a rate and cannot by itself attribute change to a cause. ❗ **No programme effect is asserted anywhere in this file**, positively or negatively.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** *"Geographical features and their location-changes… and the effects of such changes"*, General Studies Paper I, insofar as restoration is matched to a physical process. 🔁 Policy, agronomy, engineering and species conservation are routed.
- **Verified linkage:** ✅ **2026 General Studies Paper I, Question 6** — this lesson builds the **controls** element of the prescribed route, which the ledger lists as part of the required answer, and it completes the **human amplification** element by showing the same levers run in both directions. ❗ The ledger restricts current affairs for this demand to *"policy examples only"*, and that restriction is observed: ⚠️ **no current-affairs item is the analytic spine of the owned answer**; the spine is the four-pathway matching argument.
- **Traps:** listing measures without pathways; treating afforestation as a universal remedy; forgetting that canal irrigation creates the chemical pathway; using the circulating corridor-length figure; summing the three restoration instruments; citing a pledge as an achievement; quoting a publication of an institution that could not be reached; teaching conservation agronomy that belongs elsewhere.
- **Answer use:** the matching sentence is the owned answer's closing move. *"Controls must be matched to pathways: mulch squares and shelterbelts interrupt the saltation cascade and treat the aeolian pathway; controlled grazing with new pastures treats the vegetative pathway that enables the others; water harvesting treats the hydric; and only drainage with leaching treats the chemical pathway that canal irrigation itself creates — which is why the Indira Gandhi Canal turned sandy desert into a granary while giving birth to salinity and alkalinity."*

Revision notes:

- ❗ **The principle:** a measure is **matched or mismatched to a pathway at a place**. ❗ Never list measures pathway-blind.
- ✅ **Khullar's five measures (folio 212):** intensive tree plantation · **CAZRI mulch squares** · controlled grazing with **new pastures** · ban on indiscriminate felling · **alternative fuels**.
- ✅ **The mulching passage:** shifting dunes in **Bikaner, Barmer, Churu, Jaisalmer and Jhunjhunun** cover *"an approximate area of 74 thousand sq km"*; **Central Arid Zone Research Institute, Jodhpur** suggested mulching *"with different plant species"*; mulches *"are put in small squares and serve as an effective physical barrier to the moving sand."* ❗ Source-era figure; ❗ the Institute's own site **could not be reached** and **no CAZRI publication is quoted**.
- ⚠️ **Mechanisms supplied from Arc A:** plantation → 🔁 L3 rungs two and three · mulch squares → 🔁 L4 **saltation interruption** · grazing control → 🔁 L9 **pavement lag** plus 🔁 L3 cover · felling ban and alternative fuels → vegetative, demand-side.
- ❗ **The canal trade-off:** ✅ *"Although Indira Gandhi canal in Rajasthan has turned the sandy desert into a granary, it has given birth to serious problems of salinity and alkalinity."* ✅ Majid: *"Longest Canal: Indira Gandhi Canal, 682 km in length."*
- ❗ **Irrigation does not import salt; it RELOCATES stored salt upward** (🔁 L9, L12). ❗ **Drainage with leaching is the only strongly matched measure in the chemical column.**
- 📰 ✅ **Aravalli Green Wall (CA-3, 25 Mar 2023):** **5 km buffer**, **Haryana, Rajasthan, Gujarat, Delhi**, landscape *"span over 6 million hectares"*; **native species on scrubland, wasteland and degraded forest land**; **pond, lake and stream rejuvenation**; **agroforestry and pasture development**; **National Action Plan to Combat Desertification and Land Degradation Through Forestry Interventions** and an **ICFRE agroforestry FAQ** the same day.
- ❗ **[N11] "1,400 km" is EXCLUDED** — absent from the first-party release. ❗ **No corridor length anywhere in this file.** ❗ **No outcome is claimed for any programme.**
- ❗ **[U11] Three instruments, never summed:** 📰 ✅ **Bonn Challenge** — Paris 2015, **13 mha by 2020 + 8 mha by 2030 = 21 mha**, *"increased to 26 mha"* at **UNCCD COP14, 2019**; reporting assigned to **IUCN-India**. 📰 ✅ **Land Degradation Neutrality** — *"restoration of 26 Million ha of degraded land by 2030"*; 🔁 Convention reporting routed. 📰 ✅ **Twenty Point Programme** — afforestation *"approximately 18.94 million hectares during the period 2011-12 to 2021-22"*. ❗ **No fetched document reconciles them.**
- 📰 ✅ **National Forest Policy, 1988:** *"a minimum of one-third of the total land area under forest or tree cover."* ✅ Khullar's antecedent: **20-25 %** norm *"raised to 33 per cent in the second five year plan; the proportion being 20 per cent for the plains and 60 per cent for hilly and mountainous regions."* ❗ A **cover norm is not a degradation measurement**.
- 📰 ✅ Named, not developed: **Centre of Excellence at ICFRE Dehradun** for South-South cooperation; the **SAC online portal** visualising degraded area with causal processes.
- ✅ **Mid-century framing, attributed:** **S.P. Chatterjee** — *"soil erosion is the greatest single evil to Indian agriculture and animal husbandry"*; **S.I. Kayastha** — *"with soil conservation people rise and with its destruction they fall"*; **G.T. Renner** — conservation as *"the greatest good to the largest number for the longest time"*; *"about two thirds of our arable land needs conservation measures."*
- 🔁 **Named and handed off:** afforestation norms, grazing-ground earmarking, dam construction, and **crop rotation, strip cropping, contour ploughing, terracing** — **conservation agronomy, owned by the agriculture and soils owners**.
- ❗ **Limitations:** **no measured outcome**, **no cost or survival rate**, **no recovery timescale**, **no CAZRI publication quoted**, and the **74 thousand sq km** figure carries no agency, method or date.

### Concept check

**Question:** Two districts in the Indian arid zone each receive the same restoration budget. District A lies in the western shifting-dune tract; District B lies in a canal command where cropping has expanded rapidly over two decades. The same package — mass tree plantation plus mechanical sand barriers — is proposed for both. Assess the proposal for each district and state what District B actually requires.

**Model answer:** The package is **well matched in District A and largely mismatched in District B**, and the reason is that the dominant degradation pathway differs. In **District A** the pathway is **aeolian**. Mechanical barriers placed in small squares, as the Central Arid Zone Research Institute recommended for the shifting dunes of Bikaner, Barmer, Churu, Jaisalmer and Jhunjhunun, work by interrupting the saltation cascade at the surface: they prevent the chain of grain impacts from multiplying, which is what makes aeolian transport self-amplifying. Plantation then raises cover, which absorbs wind momentum before it reaches the ground, binds the substrate and supplies litter. Both components therefore act on the operative mechanism, though plantation survival under twenty-five centimetres of highly variable rainfall is itself a risk requiring protection and water. In **District B** the dominant pathway is **chemical**. Canal irrigation in an arid command is strongly positive in three respects — it adds water to a water-limited system, raises biomass and fixes dunes — but it raises the water table, and the salt that then appears at the surface was already present in the profile and the shallow groundwater, accumulated over a long arid history. Capillary rise with high evaporative demand relocates it upward. Khullar records exactly this outcome: the canal turned the sandy desert into a granary while giving birth to serious problems of salinity and alkalinity. Neither plantation nor sand barriers lowers a water table or removes salt from a root zone, so the package addresses a pathway that is no longer dominant there. What District B requires is **drainage with leaching**, together with water management that prevents the table from rising further — and that is the only column in which drainage is the strongly matched measure.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat tree planting as a universal remedy for land degradation. It is strongly matched to the vegetative and aeolian pathways and largely irrelevant to the chemical one, and in a waterlogged saline command it addresses neither the water table nor the salt store that constitute the problem.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "Restoration fails when the measure is not matched to the process." Examine with reference to India's arid zone. *(Examine — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (about 150 words):** Dryland degradation runs through four pathways — wind erosion, water erosion, salinisation and vegetation loss — and each demands a different intervention. Mulch squares, which the Central Arid Zone Research Institute recommended for the shifting dunes of western Rajasthan, interrupt the saltation cascade and treat only the aeolian pathway. Shelterbelts reduce near-ground wind speed, since abrasion and transport are concentrated close to the surface. Controlled grazing with new pastures treats the vegetative pathway, which enables the others by removing cover and breaking the pavement lag. Water harvesting treats the hydric pathway. Only drainage with leaching treats the chemical pathway — and that pathway is created by the remedy itself, since Khullar records that the Indira Gandhi Canal turned sandy desert into a granary while giving birth to serious salinity and alkalinity. The qualification is that no measured outcome for any Indian programme is available, so effectiveness remains open.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for the four pathways named and each tied to a distinct matched measure; 1 for the saltation-interruption mechanism given for a named physical measure; 3 for the canal case developed as a trade-off, including that irrigation relocates rather than imports salt; 2 for drainage identified as the only strongly matched chemical-column measure; 1 for the no-measured-outcome qualification. Deduct 3 for an undifferentiated list of measures with no pathway attached.

| **Great Victoria** | *"Large Australian desert"* | Subtropical subsidence |
| **Thar** | *"Monsoon-margin hot desert"* | ❗ **Multi-control** (🔁 **L10**) |

❗ **The two confusable pairs the map bank flags explicitly**, both of which are standing traps:

- ✅ **Sahara against Sahel** — *"Hot desert versus semi-arid transition belt."* ❗ The Sahel is **not a desert**; it is the **dryland margin**, which is exactly where 🔁 **Lesson 15** located desertification.
- ✅ **Namib against Kalahari** — *"Coastal cold-current desert versus interior semi-arid basin."* ❗ Two adjacent southern African drylands with **different causes**.

⚠️ Three further distinctions follow from the table and are worth memorising as pairs: **Gobi against Taklamakan** (north-east against the Tarim Basin); **Atacama against Patagonian** (Pacific coast against east of the southern Andes); and **Thar against Great Rann** (dune-bearing hot desert against salt marsh).

### 3. 📰 The Aravalli, mapped as the state now defines it

📰 ✅ **CA-4** supplies something no textbook does: a **definition with a measurable criterion**, adopted across states.

- ✅ The Aravallis are recognised **across 37 districts** by state governments, *"with their ecological role noted as a natural barrier against northern desertification and a protector of biodiversity and water recharge."*
- ✅ Only **Rajasthan** previously had a formal mining-regulation definition, based on its **2002 Committee Report** applying the **Richard Murphy landform classification** — *"all landforms rising 100 m above local relief are hills"* — in force *"since 09.01.2006"*.
- ✅ **All States agreed to adopt the uniform criterion of "100 metres above local relief"**.
- ✅ The range itself *"has been explained as all the landforms which exists within 500 metres of two adjoining Hills of height 100 metre or more"*, and all landforms within that **500 metre zone**, *"irrespective of their height and slopes"*, are excluded from mining leases. ✅ A buffer of **no mining within 1.0 kilometre** applies.
- ✅ The Committee was constituted per an order of **9 May 2024**, with further directions of **12 August 2025**, comprising forest secretaries of **Delhi, Haryana, Rajasthan and Gujarat** with the **Forest Survey of India**, the **Central Empowered Committee** and the **Geological Survey of India**.

❗ **Why a mapwork lesson carries a legal definition.** ⚠️ Because **the Aravalli's map outline is contested, and the contest is about the criterion**. ❗ A range defined as *"landforms rising 100 metres above local relief, clustered where hills lie within 500 metres of each other"* is a **rule for drawing a boundary**, not a boundary. ⚠️ ❗ That is the same methodological finding as 🔁 **Lesson 10's** four Thar areas and 🔁 **Lesson 11's** three-or-seven-or-225 regionalisations, now in a judicial register: **a geographical unit's extent depends on the criterion adopted.**

❗ **Dating discipline.** ⚠️ This account is as of **21 December 2025**. ❗ **Any later development is outside this edition's evidence and is not asserted.**

| 🔁 **A7** | **2026 Prelims, Question 39** — a desert lake's geography, status and heritage listing | **Geography**, `basic/09_Lakes.md` | ⚠️ Named at 🔁 **L12** as the boundary with the lakes owner. ❗ **The locally held 2026 key is explicitly provisional** |

❗ **The answer-neutrality rule, restated because this is the lesson where temptation is highest.** ⚠️ **No correct option is stated, implied or converged upon for any of these.** ❗ **No elimination chain is written.** ❗ **The provisional 2026 key is not converted into an assertion.** ⚠️ These demands are cited **to show what the examiner asks about**, never **what the answer was**.

### 6. 🇮🇳 India example and its limitation — the drill, and what it cannot do

⚠️ **The drill.** ❗ Draw the outline; mark the **Aravalli axis south-west to north-east**; place the **Luni** flowing south-west to the **Rann**; mark the **Sambhar cluster** east of the Marusthali and west of Jaipur; mark **Barmer, Jaisalmer, Bikaner, Churu and Jhunjhunun** in the shifting-dune tract; mark **Jaipur and Sikar** on the low-dune eastern side; place the **Rann of Kachchh** and **Sir Creek** to the south-west; mark the **Indira Gandhi Canal** across the western command; and draw the **25 centimetre isohyet** and the **limit of wind erosion** as **dashed lines with a note that both are class boundaries, not features**.

❗ **The limitations, four of them.**

- ❗ **The map bank has no row for the Indira Gandhi Canal, the Marusthali, the Rajasthan Bagar, Barmer or Jaisalmer.** ⚠️ Each is placed from Khullar's folios 76 to 77 and 211 with the page cited, and ❗ **the absence is recorded in the source ledger.**
- ❗ **Two of the lines on this map are not features.** ⚠️ The **25 centimetre isohyet** is a climatic average that **moves between years** — acutely so at a monsoon margin (🔁 **L10**). The **limit of wind erosion** is a **mapped class boundary from one book figure**, with no stated method. ❗ Neither should be drawn as a hard line without that note.
- ❗ **The Aravalli's outline depends on the criterion** (section 3), and its legal definition is dated **21 December 2025**.
- ❗ **No coordinates, no areas and no widths** are given for the Marusthali, the thali or the Bagar in any source available to this edition (🔁 **L11**). ⚠️ The map is a **sequence**, correct in order and relative position, and is not a measured map.

### Comparison, objection and the residual

| Line type on this map | Example | ❗ What it means | ❗ What it does not mean |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Physical feature** | Aravalli axis; Luni course | A thing on the ground | — |
| **Administrative / recognised unit** | ✅ Aravallis across **37 districts**; the **100 m above local relief** criterion | A unit defined by an adopted rule, dated | A natural boundary |
| **Climatic class boundary** | 25 cm isohyet | An average position of a rainfall value | A fixed line, or a wall |
| **Process class boundary** | ✅ *"LIMIT OF WIND EROSION"* on Fig. 7.2 | Where a mapped process class was drawn to end | ❗ A frontier that advances (🔁 **L13, L15**) |
| **Infrastructure** | ✅ Indira Gandhi Canal, **682 km** | A built alignment and its command | A physiographic division |

**Strongest objection.** This topic owns **zero** previous-year objective demands. A mapwork lesson in a Mains edition with no owned objective route is weight that could have gone to argument.

**Reply.** Three reasons, and the ledger supplies all three. ❗ **First, four of the seven adjacent demands are location or identification demands**, so the examiner demonstrably asks locational questions about this material even though another owner answers them. ❗ **Second, the owned Mains demand is spatial in its own terms**: *desertification and land degradation* are reported **by state and by process**, and an answer that cannot say **where** is an answer that cannot use India's own evidence. ⚠️ 🔁 **Lesson 14's** decisive observation — that Uttar Pradesh leads both the ravine table and the salinity table while Rajasthan leads neither — **is a map statement**. ❗ **Third, the confusable pairs are the topic's cheapest marks and its cheapest errors**, and they are recorded explicitly in the repository's own map bank.

⚠️ **Residual.** ❗ **No map in this edition is drawn to a measured scale**, and none should be reproduced as if it were. ⚠️ The sketch above is a **relative-position drill at the national-atlas scale**, and the Basic owner's scale control forbids mixing it with any other scale — ❗ which is why no dune, no grain and no basin cross-section appears on it.

### UPSC application

- **Syllabus:** *"Geographical features and their location-changes"*, General Studies Paper I, and the physical-geography clause. 🔁 Protected-area network, lakes, world regional identification and xerophyte physiology are routed by name.
- **Verified linkage:** ✅ **2026 General Studies Paper I, Question 6** — this lesson makes the owned answer **place-specific**, which is the difference between a generic process answer and one grounded in India's own mapped evidence. 🔁 **A3, A5, A6, A7** named by year and number and routed; ❗ **no key recorded or inferred for any of them.**
- **Traps:** drawing the Aravalli east-west; sending the Luni to the Arabian Sea; calling the Rann a sand desert; placing Desert National Park in Kutch; treating the 25 centimetre isohyet or the limit of wind erosion as fixed features; confusing the Sahara with the Sahel or the Namib with the Kalahari; adding bustard or habitat content to a location.
- **Answer use:** a Mains answer rarely needs a drawn map, but it always needs **placement in words**. *"Degradation in India's arid zone is not uniformly distributed: the shifting-dune tract of Bikaner, Barmer, Churu, Jaisalmer and Jhunjhunun carries the aeolian pathway; the canal command west of the Aravalli carries the chemical pathway; and the ravine and salinity tables are led by Uttar Pradesh rather than by Rajasthan, which is why a national answer cannot be a Rajasthan answer."*

Revision notes:

- ❗ **Scale rule:** this map is drawn at the **national atlas scale only**. ❗ No dune, grain or basin section appears on it.
- ✅ **Thar** — *"Monsoon-margin hot desert"*, India-Pakistan, west Rajasthan-Sindh. ❗ *"Great Rann is a salt marsh."*
- ✅ **Aravalli** — SW-NE, *"800 km from Delhi to Ahmedabad"*; *"desert barrier and mineral belt"*. ❗ *"Runs SW-NE, unlike the Vindhya-Satpura east-west tendency."*
- ✅ **Luni** — Aravallis to the Rann, flowing **south-west**. ❗ *"Does not normally reach the Arabian Sea as a perennial river."*
- ✅ **Sambhar** — **65 km west of Jaipur**, altitude **~360 m**, **~225 sq km** in the rains; with **Didwana, Degana, Kuchaman, Sargol, Khatu**. ❗ *"Not a coastal lagoon."*
- ✅ **Rann of Kutch** — Great and Little; *"Seasonal salt marsh, seismicity and flamingo habitat"*. ❗ *"Not a conventional sand desert."* ✅ **Sir Creek** — *"a tidal estuary, not a river boundary throughout."*
- ✅ **Sabarmati** — *"Not sourced in the Western Ghats."* ✅ **Khetri** (copper, Jhunjhunu) against **Zawar** (zinc-lead, south of Udaipur). ✅ **Hemis** — Ladakh cold desert, *"Not in Himachal Pradesh."*
- ✅ **Desert National Park** — Jaisalmer-Barmer. ❗ *"Not the Rann of Kutch."* ❗ **A location only — no bustard, habitat or conservation content anywhere in this file.**
- ❗ **No map-bank row exists** for the **Indira Gandhi Canal** (✅ **682 km**, Majid printed 10.5), **Marusthali**, **Rajasthan Bagar**, **Barmer** or **Jaisalmer**. ❗ Each placed from **Khullar folios 76-77 and 211**, with the absence disclosed in the source ledger.
- ❗ **Two lines are not features:** the **25 cm isohyet** (a moving climatic average) and the dashed **"LIMIT OF WIND EROSION"** on Fig. 7.2 (a mapped class boundary, ❗ **not an advancing frontier**).
- ✅ **World deserts that earn a place:** Sahara · **Sahel** (the margin, not a desert) · Kalahari · **Namib** (cold current) · Arabian · **Gobi** (continental) · Taklamakan · **Atacama** (cold current) · **Patagonian** (rain shadow) · Great Victoria · **Thar** (multi-control) · ❗ **Confusable pairs:** **Sahara / Sahel** · **Namib / Kalahari** · Gobi / Taklamakan · Atacama / Patagonian · **Thar / Great Rann** · **Desert National Park / Rann of Kutch**.
- 📰 ✅ **CA-4, 21 Dec 2025:** Aravallis across **37 districts**; **"100 metres above local relief"** criterion adopted by all States, from Rajasthan's **2002 Committee Report** on the **Richard Murphy** classification, in force since **09.01.2006**; range = landforms *"within 500 metres of two adjoining Hills of height 100 metre or more"*; **no mining within 1.0 km**; Committee per order of **9 May 2024**, further directions **12 Aug 2025**, with **Delhi, Haryana, Rajasthan, Gujarat** forest secretaries plus **FSI, CEC, GSI**. ❗ Nothing after 21 Dec 2025 is asserted.
- ❗ **A boundary rule is not a boundary** — the same finding as L10's four areas and L11's rival regionalisations.
- ✅ **Five figures:** **3.13** Great Plain regional divisions · **3.16** Physiographic Divisions (WESTERN PLAINS) · **7.2** Soil Erosion (**six classes**, aeolian one of them, plus the limit line) · **7.3** Ravines · **7.4** Saline and Alkaline Lands. ❗ The last three are the **limiting argument in map form**.
- 🔁 **Routed, named by year and number, no key recorded or inferred:** **A3** 2018 Prelims Q2 (Science and Technology) · **A5** 2020 Prelims Q95 (Environment and Ecology, protected areas) · **A6** 2022 Prelims Q23 (Topic 34) · **A7** 2026 Prelims Q39 (Topic 09, ❗ **provisional** key).

### Concept check

**Question:** An answer script contains this sentence: "The Aravalli range runs east-west across Rajasthan, blocking the Thar, while the Luni drains the desert westward into the Arabian Sea and the Rann of Kachchh forms the desert's sandy southern extension." Identify every locational and conceptual error, and give a corrected sentence.

**Model answer:** There are four errors, three locational and one conceptual. **First, the alignment.** The Aravalli runs **south-west to north-east**, from near Ahmedabad toward Delhi over about eight hundred kilometres; the map bank flags this explicitly as the distinction from the Vindhya-Satpura east-west tendency. The error matters beyond orientation: a south-west to north-east range lies **along** the monsoon flow rather than across it, which is why it supplies little forced ascent. **Second, "blocking".** The range is best described as the **eastern limit of continuous dune cover** and a topographic and ecological transition coinciding with a rainfall gradient; its measurable functions are local wind reduction, sand trapping, seasonal stream supply and groundwater recharge. It is not a wall against a moving frontier, since degradation is capability loss in place. **Third, the Luni's direction and destination.** It flows **south-west to the Rann of Kachchh**, not westward to the sea, and the map bank records that it does not normally reach the Arabian Sea as a perennial river. **Fourth, the Rann.** It is regionally continuous with the arid tract and is the Luni's terminus, but it is geomorphically a **seasonally inundated saline mudflat and marsh**, which the map bank records as not a conventional sand desert; calling it a sandy extension merges two different landform systems. A corrected sentence: *"The Aravalli runs south-west to north-east for about eight hundred kilometres from near Ahmedabad toward Delhi, marking the eastern limit of continuous dune cover; the seasonal Luni flows south-west from the range to the Rann of Kachchh, which is a saline mudflat and marsh rather than a dune field."*

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat a line on a thematic map as a physical boundary. The twenty-five centimetre isohyet is a moving climatic average, and the dashed limit of wind erosion on Khullar's Fig. 7.2 marks where a mapped process class was drawn to end; neither is a frontier, and neither should be reproduced as a hard line without that qualification.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "In India's arid zone, where a feature lies decides what kind of degradation it suffers." Discuss with located examples. *(Discuss — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (about 150 words):** Location and pathway track one another closely. In the shifting-dune tract of Bikaner, Barmer, Churu, Jaisalmer and Jhunjhunun, west of the Aravalli beyond the twenty-five centimetre isohyet, mean dune heights reach about thirty metres and the aeolian pathway dominates. On the eastern Marusthali, where gneiss, schist and granite outcrops show Peninsular bedrock beneath, surfaces are armoured by a pebble lag and the risk is breaking that armour. In the semi-arid Rajasthan Bagar up to the Aravalli, with steppe vegetation and cultivated rohi patches drained by seasonal Aravalli streams, cover loss and water erosion dominate. In the Indira Gandhi Canal command the pathway is chemical, since a raised water table relocates stored salt upward. Nationally, Khullar's tables place ravines and salinity highest in Uttar Pradesh, not Rajasthan. The qualification is that two of these boundaries, the isohyet and the mapped limit of wind erosion, are class lines rather than features.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 2 for at least three correctly located Indian tracts with their place names; 2 for each tract matched to its dominant degradation pathway; 2 for the national comparison showing the leading state differs by pathway; 3 for at least three correct distinguishing statements drawn from the confusable pairs, including the Aravalli alignment, the Luni's terminus and the Rann's character; 1 for the qualification that isohyets and mapped process limits are class boundaries, not features. Deduct 2 for any statement that the Luni reaches the Arabian Sea.

   bounds it, [B]-[D] deliver the demanded content, [E] completes the
