One question, six blocks, a word budget that adds to 150. Everything in Lessons 1 to 17 exists to make these six blocks writable without hesitation.

### 1. The demand, exactly as the ledger carries it

✅ **2026, General Studies Paper I, Question 6:** *"Discuss the role of aeolian processes in desertification and land degradation."* ✅ **10 marks / 150 words.**

✅ **Primary Basic owner:** `Geography/basic/07_Arid-Desert-Landforms.md`. ✅ **Support owner:** `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/23_Desertification-UNCCD-and-Land-Degradation.md`. ✅ **Required answer route**, in the ledger's own words: *"Deflation, abrasion, saltation, dust transport and deposition; human amplification; land-productivity effects and controls."* ✅ **Current-affairs role:** *"Policy examples only."* ✅ **Readiness: FULL.**

❗ **Two features of the ledger entry shape everything below.** ⚠️ **First, the route is prescribed**, and it names **five physical terms** plus **three analytic requirements**. An answer that omits one of the eight has omitted something the ledger says the question requires. ❗ **Second, current affairs is explicitly bounded to illustration.** ⚠️ The analytic spine must be the **mechanism**, not a headline, and the model below observes that strictly: its only current-affairs element is a **dated national figure used as an evidence anchor**, in the last block.

### 2. Decoding the directive — what *discuss* asks for at ten marks

⚠️ The Basic owner's §9.1 decoding table supplies the general principle; this section applies it to this question.

| The verb | What it demands at 10 marks | ❗ What it does not demand |
|---|---|---|
| **Discuss** | A **bounded argument**: define the field, set out the mechanism, state the consequence, and **qualify** | ❗ An exhaustive inventory. A list of every aeolian landform would **lose** marks at this length |
| **"the role of"** | ⚠️ A **relative** claim — the role of aeolian processes **among** the processes at work | ❗ A claim that aeolian processes are the whole story |
| **"in desertification and land degradation"** | ⚠️ ❗ **Both terms are used, separately and deliberately.** The answer must show it knows they are not synonyms | ❗ Treating them as one word |

❗ **The decisive inference, and it is the single most valuable observation in this lesson:** ⚠️ **the examiner wrote "the role of", which is a request for proportion.** ❗ An answer that supplies the mechanism **and its bound** answers the question asked; an answer that supplies the mechanism alone answers a question about aeolian processes in general.

### 3. The nine required elements, and where each was built

❗ **Gate check: every element of the ledger's prescribed route is built somewhere in Lessons 1 to 17 and assembled here.**

| # | Required element | 🔁 Built at | What the answer uses |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Deflation** | **L4** (process), **L5** (landforms), **L9** (pavement) | Removal of the fine fraction; the lag it leaves; blowouts where armour breaks |
| 2 | **Abrasion** | **L5** | Tool-dependent; concentrated near the ground; the height-zonation result |
| 3 | **Saltation** | **L4** | The **self-amplifying cascade**: each landing grain ejects several more |
| 4 | **Dust transport** | **L4**, **L7** | Suspension carries most of the load; export beyond the system; loess as proof |
| 5 | **Deposition** | **L6**, **L7** | Dune form as a readout of supply and directionality; sand onto cropland; loess at the far-field sink |
| 6 | **Human amplification** | **L3**, **L9**, **L13**, **L14** | Cover removal, grazing, tillage, broken pavement — ❗ **with the owner's caveat that people are not inherently a degradation driver** |
| 7 | **Land-productivity effects** | **L7**, **L9**, **L14** | The exported fraction carries clay, organic matter and nutrients; loss is irreversible at human timescales |
| 8 | **Controls** | **L16** | ❗ **Matched to pathways**: mulch squares and shelterbelts interrupt saltation; grazing control restores the enabling cover |
| 9 | **The limiting argument** | **L8**, **L14**, **L15** | Aeolian is **one of four pathways**; India's own ravine and salinity tables are led by Uttar Pradesh, not Rajasthan |

❗ **The integration claim, verifiable in this table:** elements 1 to 5 and 7 are built in **Arc A** world-process lessons; elements 6, 8 and 9 draw on **Arc B** India lessons; and **element 9 draws on both** — 🔁 **Lesson 8** for the world-process hydric argument and 🔁 **Lesson 14** for the Indian numbers. ⚠️ That is why this edition is one sequence and not two documents.

### 4. The model answer

**Original model answer for the 2026 demand (about 150 words):**

> Desertification is land degradation in arid, semi-arid and dry sub-humid areas, so a desert cannot desertify; the margin is at risk. Aeolian processes act through a chain. Deflation strips fines; saltating sand abrades near the ground and, because each landing grain ejects several more, transport is self-amplifying; suspension exports silt and clay beyond the region entirely; deposition buries cropland and mobilises dunes. The productivity loss is severe because the exported fraction carries the clay, organic matter and nutrients, and is irrecoverable at human timescales. Human action amplifies this by changing the surface, not the climate — removing cover, concentrating grazing, breaking the protective pavement lag — though cover and rainfall variability interact differently by site. Controls must match the pathway: mulch squares and shelterbelts interrupt saltation, grazing management restores cover. Critically, aeolian action is one of four pathways within India's reported 97.84 million hectares of degraded land for 2018-19.

❗ **Word count: 149 words.** ⚠️ Counted, not estimated, and counted twice.

**Rubric for the owned demand (quantified, 10 marks):** 2 for the definitional frame placing desertification in the dryland class and stating that the core cannot desertify; 3 for the aeolian chain — deflation, abrasion, saltation, suspension, deposition — given as causal sequence with the self-amplification named; 2 for the productivity mechanism identified as **export of the fine, nutrient-bearing fraction** rather than mere thinning; 1 for human amplification stated as a **surface** change with the owner's caveat attached; 1 for at least one control **matched** to the aeolian pathway by mechanism; 1 for the limiting argument with a dated, attributed national figure. ❗ Deduct 2 for treating desert and desertification as synonyms; deduct 2 for any claim of a desert advancing at a stated rate; deduct 1 for an undated or unattributed hectare figure.

### 5. Dissection — why each sentence is there, and what was left out

| Block | The sentence, abbreviated | ❗ Why it earns its words | ❗ What was deliberately omitted |
|---|---|---|---|
| **[A]** | *"Desertification is land degradation in arid, semi-arid and dry sub-humid areas, so a desert cannot desertify; the margin is at risk."* | ⚠️ Bounds the whole answer in one move and pre-empts the topic's highest-value trap. 🔁 **L15** | ❗ The convention's name and architecture — **routed** |
| **[B]** | The chain: deflation, abrasion, saltation with self-amplification, suspension, deposition | ⚠️ Delivers **five of the ledger's named route terms** in causal order. 🔁 **L4, L5, L6, L7** | ❗ Every landform name — zeugen, yardang, ventifact, barchan, seif, loess. ⚠️ **At 150 words, naming landforms displaces mechanism** |
| **[C]** | *"the exported fraction carries the clay, organic matter and nutrients, and is irrecoverable at human timescales"* | ⚠️ Converts erosion into **capability loss**, which is what the question's second noun requires. 🔁 **L7, L9** | ❗ The soil-profile apparatus; the Aridisol name |
| **[D]** | *"changing the surface, not the climate"*, with the interaction caveat | ⚠️ Delivers the **human amplification** element and the owner's causal control in one clause. 🔁 **L3, L13** | ❗ The origin dispute; the advancing-desert rate; all four reported statements |
| **[E]** | *"Controls must match the pathway"* with two matched instances | ⚠️ Delivers the **controls** element and demonstrates **why** each works. 🔁 **L16** | ❗ Every pledge, programme name and institutional fact. ⚠️ **Policy examples only, and at this length, none** |
| **[F]** | *"one of four pathways within India's reported 97.84 million hectares… for 2018-19"* | ⚠️ The **limiting argument** plus a dated, attributed anchor, in one sentence. 🔁 **L14, L15** | ❗ Tables 7.1, 7.2, 7.3; the 21-against-45 audit; the two-epoch caveat |

❗ **The hardest editorial decision, stated openly.** ⚠️ 🔁 **Lesson 14's** audit — the strongest methodological material in this edition — **does not survive into the 150-word answer**, because the question asks about a process, not about evidence quality. ❗ It survives instead as the **reason the only figure quoted is the dated, attributed one**. ⚠️ **Good source criticism shows up in an answer as restraint, not as commentary.**

### 6. Three failure modes, and the sentence that prevents each

| Failure mode | What it looks like | ❗ The preventing sentence |
|---|---|---|
| **The landform catalogue** | Deflation hollows, zeugen, yardangs, ventifacts, dreikanter, barchans, seifs, loess — then no words left for degradation | ❗ *"Aeolian processes act through a chain"* — commit to **process**, and the landforms become unnecessary |
| **The synonym collapse** | *Desertification* and *desert* used interchangeably; the answer drifts into describing a desert | ❗ Block **[A]**, written first |
| **The unbounded claim** | Aeolian processes presented as the cause of land degradation in India | ❗ Block **[F]**, written last and never cut |

### 8. 🇮🇳 India example and its limitation — the precision ceiling on this answer

⚠️ **The strongest India-specific sentence available for this demand**, and it is deliberately not in the model answer: ❗ **India reports 97.84 million hectares of land degradation and desertification for 2018-19, and its own national portal attributes degraded area to the processes causing it — but the aeolian share of that total is not available to this edition.**

❗ **The limitation, stated as a ceiling.** ⚠️ The ideal answer to this question would say *"aeolian processes account for X per cent of India's degraded area"*. ❗ **No source available to this edition supplies X.** ⚠️ The publication carrying the process breakdown was **not downloaded**; the Ministry release gives the total but not the split; and the advanced owner's statement that **wind erosion drives more than 40 per cent of Rajasthan's desertification** is a **single-state statement resting on that owner alone**, used with the owner named and the period given, ❗ **with no percentage attached to Rajasthan's total anywhere in this file.**

❗ **What the answer does instead**, and it is defensible: ⚠️ it states **the number of pathways** rather than the share of one, and it supports the limitation with 🔁 **Lesson 14's** state-resolved tables, in which **Uttar Pradesh leads both ravines and salinity while Rajasthan leads neither**. ⚠️ ❗ **That is a structural argument rather than a quantitative one, and the structural argument is the one the evidence supports.**

📰 ✅ **The international frame, named once if a sentence can be spared at longer length:** the desertification Convention's **COP16 took place in Riyadh from 2 to 13 December 2024**, under the theme *"Our Land. Our Future."* 🔁 Everything beyond naming it is **routed**.

### Comparison, objection and the residual

**The comparison that completes the file is the owner's reusable spine against this question's spine.**

| | ✅ The owner's reusable 10-mark spine — *"Why are deserts located where they are?"* | ⚠️ This question's spine — *"the role of aeolian processes in desertification and land degradation"* |
|---|---|---|
| Opening move | **Thesis**: desert location is the surface expression of circulation | **Definition**: desertification is degradation **restricted to drylands** |
| Body | Cause-by-cause, each anchored to a named desert | **Chain**: deflation, abrasion, saltation, suspension, deposition |
| The consequence step | Aridity → sparse vegetation → exposed surface → both aeolian and fluvial suites | **Export of the fine fraction → capability loss** |
| The qualification | Causes overlap; sand is a minority surface; cold deserts are largest | ❗ **Aeolian is one of four pathways** |
| Closing | Deserts as a **global circulation output**; margins are where variability and pressure interact | ❗ The **margin**, not the core, is where degradation is measured |
| ❗ The common structure | ⚠️ **Thesis → mechanism → consequence → qualification → relocation to the margin.** Both spines share it, which is why the owner's spine is genuinely reusable |

**Strongest objection.** A whole lesson devoted to one 150-word answer is disproportionate. The same effort spread across the preceding seventeen lessons would teach more geography.

**Reply.** ❗ The 150-word constraint **is** the geography, because it forces the selection that distinguishes understanding from coverage. ⚠️ Seventeen lessons produced perhaps forty candidate sentences; the exercise here is deciding which six blocks survive and **why**, and the *why* is in every case a judgement about **what the examiner's verb and nouns demand**. ❗ More practically: this topic owns **one** Mains demand across nine years of ledgers. ⚠️ Everything else in this file is **transferable capability** — it feeds answers owned by other files and questions not yet asked — but **this one answer is the file's own deliverable**, and under-building it would be the single clearest way to waste the preceding seventeen lessons.

⚠️ **Residual.** ❗ **Three things this answer cannot do on the available evidence**, each stated rather than concealed: it cannot give **the aeolian share** of India's degraded area; it cannot give **a rate** of degradation, because the only national change evidence is a two-epoch comparison; and it cannot give **a recovery timescale** for any restoration measure, because none exists in any source consulted. ⚠️ **An answer that quietly supplies any of the three would be stronger-sounding and weaker.**

### Concept check

**Question:** Two candidates answer the 2026 demand. Candidate A writes 150 words naming and describing deflation hollows, desert pavement, zeugens, yardangs, ventifacts, barchans, seifs and loess, concluding that wind is the principal agent of desertification in India. Candidate B writes 150 words defining desertification, setting out deflation, saltation, suspension and deposition as a chain, explaining that the exported fine fraction carries the nutrients, noting that human action changes the surface rather than the climate, matching two controls to the aeolian pathway, and closing by saying that aeolian action is one of four pathways within India's reported degraded area for 2018-19. Mark both and explain the difference.

**Model answer:** Candidate B scores substantially higher, and the reasons are structural rather than a matter of style. **On the ledger's prescribed route**, which names deflation, abrasion, saltation, dust transport and deposition, plus human amplification, land-productivity effects and controls, Candidate A delivers only the first group and does so as **nouns rather than as processes**; Candidate B delivers all eight. **On the verb**, *discuss* at ten marks rewards a bounded argument, and *"the role of"* is a request for **proportion**; Candidate A supplies no proportion and then overstates it in the conclusion, which is the costliest error in the script. **On the second noun**, the question says *desertification and land degradation*, two terms the examiner has deliberately kept distinct; Candidate A never defines either, so the answer describes a desert rather than a degradation process, and never reaches the capability-loss argument that makes the question answerable. Candidate B's definitional opening also pre-empts the standing trap that a desert cannot desertify, since the at-risk land is the productive margin. **On evidence**, Candidate B attaches one dated, attributed national figure; Candidate A attaches none. **On qualification**, Candidate B's closing sentence is the single highest-value line in either script, because it converts a description into an assessment of relative role — which is exactly what was asked. Candidate A's closing sentence does the opposite: it is an unbounded claim contradicted by India's own tables, in which ravines and salinity are led by Uttar Pradesh rather than by Rajasthan.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not assume that naming more technical terms demonstrates more knowledge. At 150 words, every landform name displaces a clause of mechanism, and the examiner's marks sit on the mechanism and its bound, not on the vocabulary.

**Original Mains practice [10 marks]:** "Wind is the most visible agent of land degradation in drylands, but not necessarily the most important." Examine. *(Examine — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model (about 150 words):** The distinction between visibility and importance is well founded. Aeolian action is conspicuous: dust storms are seen, dunes encroach on fields, and sand burying cropland is immediately legible. Its mechanisms are real: deflation strips fines, saltation is self-amplifying since each landing grain ejects several more, and suspension exports clay, organic matter and nutrients beyond the region irrecoverably. But dryland degradation runs through four pathways. Water erosion produces rills, gullies and ravines; salinisation and alkalinisation follow a raised water table relocating stored salt upward; and vegetation loss enables all the others. India's own tabulations are decisive: ravines and salinity are both led by Uttar Pradesh, not Rajasthan, and the national soil-erosion map carries aeolian erosion as one of six classes. The qualification is that the pathways interact rather than compete, with cover loss as the common amplifier, so ranking them is less useful than matching each control to its process.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 4 for the aeolian mechanism set out as a causal chain with at least three stages named correctly and the self-amplification or export argument included; 4 for all four pathways identified and at least two supported with located Indian evidence; 2 for the interaction qualification replacing a simple ranking. Deduct 3 for asserting aeolian dominance without evidence and 2 for any undated or unattributed national figure.

## M1 — approach note

⚠️ **The directive.** *Discuss* at ten marks wants a **bounded, qualified argument**, not an inventory. ❗ The phrase *"the role of"* is a request for **proportion**: the role of aeolian processes **among** the processes at work. ❗ An answer that supplies the mechanism without its bound has answered a different question.

# CUMULATIVE CONCEPT CHECKS

⚠️ Four checks. Three consolidate an arc or a method; the fourth spans the whole file, which is justified because the chain from a water-balance deficit to a 150-word answer cannot be tested inside any single arc. ❗ No compiled objective corpus appears here or anywhere in this file.

### Cumulative check A — the world-process arc, Lessons 1 to 9

**Question:** Build the chain from "potential evapotranspiration exceeds precipitation" to "a hectare of dryland has permanently lost part of its productive capacity", naming every intermediate step, and identify the single point at which the loss becomes irreversible at human timescales.

**Model answer:** One: aridity is a water-balance deficit, so for most of the year the net flux of water through the soil is upward rather than downward. Two: that deficit suppresses vegetation, which elsewhere absorbs wind momentum, binds the substrate and covers it with litter. Three: an unprotected surface exposes whatever loose material weathering, flash-flood deposition or inherited deposits have supplied, provided it is dry and uncohesive. Four: once a threshold wind speed is exceeded, sand enters saltation, and because each landing grain delivers more energy to the bed than it needed to leave it, several grains are ejected per impact and the transport population multiplies. Five: that saltating sheet does three things at once — it abrades standing rock near the ground, it pushes the coarse fraction into surface creep, and it shatters cohesive aggregates in fine-grained surfaces. Six: the shattered fines enter suspension, whose settling velocity is below the upward turbulent eddies, so they remain aloft and travel far beyond the region, settling as loess at a distant sink. Seven: the coarse residue that the wind cannot lift concentrates as a lag and, once continuous, armours the surface so that deflation largely stops. Eight: arid soils are mineral-rich but organic-poor, because low productivity supplies little organic matter and high temperatures oxidise what arrives; their aggregates are therefore weak and their protection depends on that physical lag rather than on biological structure. The loss becomes irreversible at human timescales at **step six**. Everything up to step five redistributes material within the region — a dune that migrates is still local sediment, and creep concentrates rather than removes. Suspension is different in kind: it carries the clay, organic matter and nutrient-bearing fraction **out of the system entirely**, over distances of thousands of kilometres, and no process returns it. The hectare has not merely been thinned; it has lost the specific fraction on which its fertility depended.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat all three transport modes as equivalent losses. Creep and saltation move material within the dryland and can in principle be reversed by trapping and stabilisation; suspension exports it permanently, which is why dust is the mode that converts erosion into capability loss.

### Cumulative check B — the method arc, Lessons 10, 11, 13, 14 and 17

**Question:** This edition reaches the same methodological finding from five different directions. State the finding, give the five instances, and explain why the repetition is evidence rather than redundancy.

**Model answer:** The finding is that **a geographical quantity is an answer to a question, and the question is set by the criterion adopted** — so a figure without its criterion is not evidence. Five instances. **First, the Thar's area:** four figures on four pages — 260,000 square kilometres in one table, 259,000 in the same book's India extremes list, about 2.0 lakh square kilometres in total with 1.75 lakh in India in another — differing according to whether the Pakistani extension is counted, whether the semi-arid Bagar is included, and whether the eastern limit is the Aravalli or the twenty-five centimetre isohyet. **Second, physiographic regionalisation:** India divided into three major natural regions by one scholar in 1922, three macro regions with thirty-four first-order, seventy-four second-order and two hundred and twenty-five subdivisions by another, and seven divisions by a third in 1965 — the same country, three answers, three criteria. **Third, the wind-erosion contradiction:** 21 million hectares and 45 million hectares of severe wind erosion on a single folio, differing only by the addition of Western Uttar Pradesh to the state list, with no definition of "severe", no method and no date on either. **Fourth, aridity shares:** prose giving "about 30 per cent" semi-arid where the same book's own table on the facing folio gives 29.13 per cent. **Fifth, the Aravalli's extent:** a judicial process adopting a uniform criterion that any landform rising a hundred metres above local relief is a hill and that the range comprises landforms within five hundred metres of two such hills — which is a rule for drawing a boundary, not a boundary. The repetition is evidence rather than redundancy because the five cases are **independent**: two different books, a judicial process, a textbook's prose against its own table, and a century of scholarly regionalisation. A finding that recurs across independent evidence classes is a property of the subject, not an artefact of one source. The practical consequence is that this edition quotes one national figure as a figure — the Ministry-reported 97.84 million hectares for 2018-19 on the Space Applications Centre's mapping — because it is the only one carrying agency, period and a documented method.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not read this finding as scepticism about measurement in general. The argument is not that figures are unknowable but that a figure is incomplete without its definition, scope, method and period — and that the appropriate response to an incomplete figure is to say so and use a better one, not to average or to pick.

### Cumulative check C — the India application arc, Lessons 10 to 17

**Question:** Explain why a single national restoration programme, applied at uniform specification across the Indian arid zone, would succeed in some places, be redundant in others and be counter-productive in at least one — using the transect and the pathways established in this file.

**Model answer:** Four pathways run through dryland degradation — aeolian, hydric, chemical and vegetative — and their dominance changes along a west-to-east transect of about six hundred and fifty kilometres. In the **western Marusthali**, around Barmer, Jaisalmer, Bikaner, Churu and Jhunjhunun, mean dune heights reach about thirty metres, dunes shift and are locally called dhrian, and rainfall is below the twenty-five centimetre isohyet; the aeolian pathway dominates, and mulch squares placed as a physical barrier to moving sand, shelterbelts and protected plantation are well matched, because they interrupt the saltation cascade and raise cover. In the **eastern Marusthali**, bedrock outcrops of gneiss, schist and granite show Peninsular rock beneath and surfaces are armoured by a coarse lag; dune stabilisation is largely redundant there because there is little free sand, and what matters instead is **not breaking the armour** — routing grazing concentration, controlling vehicle tracks and quarry access. In the **Rajasthan Bagar**, east of the isohyet up to the Aravalli, the land is semi-arid, less sandy, more humid, clothed in steppe vegetation and cultivated in fertile rohi patches drained by short seasonal Aravalli streams, with mean dune heights falling to about eight metres near Jaipur and Sikar; here cover management, grazing control with new pastures, water harvesting and agroforestry match the vegetative and hydric pathways, while sand barriers address a pathway that is not dominant. In the **canal command**, the dominant pathway is chemical: irrigation added water to a water-limited system, raised biomass, fixed dunes — and raised the water table, so capillary rise and evaporation relocate salt that was already stored in the profile and the shallow groundwater, which is why Khullar records that the canal turned sandy desert into a granary while giving birth to serious salinity and alkalinity. More plantation there treats nothing and may raise local evapotranspiration without touching the salt store; drainage with leaching is the only strongly matched measure, and uniform irrigation expansion without drainage is the counter-productive case. The general principle is that a measure is neither good nor bad in itself — it is matched or mismatched to a pathway at a place.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not treat the canal command as simply a success with a side-effect. In pathway terms it is a three-column success and a one-column failure, and the failing column is the one that no other measure in the standard package addresses — which is why the trade-off is structural rather than incidental.

### Cumulative check D — the whole file, Lessons 1 to 18

**Question:** State what this topic establishes about the relationship between a physical process and a policy claim, drawing on at least six lessons, and say precisely where the relationship breaks down.

**Model answer:** The topic's recurring finding is that **a physical process constrains what a policy claim can mean, without determining what a policy chooses to do** — and that most confusion in this subject comes from reading a policy statement as though it were a measurement. The constraint is tight at the mechanical end. A water-balance deficit, not heat or sand, defines a desert, which is why Antarctica is one and why most desert surfaces are rock and gravel (Lessons 1 and 2). Wind does geomorphic work only where a surface supplies dry, loose, uncohesive material and nothing protects it, so the limiting variable is the ground rather than the air (Lesson 3). Transport is sorted: creep and saltation redistribute within the region while suspension exports the fine, nutrient-bearing fraction beyond it, which is the precise mechanical sense in which erosion becomes capability loss (Lessons 4 and 7). Dune form reads out sand supply and wind directionality, so a parabolic dune records a breached vegetated surface and a barchan field records supply limitation at a margin (Lesson 6). The chain then begins to loosen. Desertification is defined as land degradation restricted to the arid, semi-arid and dry sub-humid classes, so a desert cannot desertify and the at-risk land is the productive margin — a definitional constraint that rules out an entire family of claims about advancing frontiers (Lesson 15). India's own national assessment is accordingly class-based, process-attributed and state-resolved, reported as 97.84 million hectares for 2018-19 on the Space Applications Centre's mapping (Lessons 14 and 15). And here the relationship breaks down, at a specific and nameable point: **where a programme objective or a judicial observation is written in the register of a measurement**. The Aravalli Green Wall's stated objective is to prevent the eastward expansion of the Thar; the Supreme Court is recorded as emphasising the range as a barrier against desertification; a standard textbook asserts advance at about half a kilometre a year. The first two are genuine, dated, first-party statements of why public action was taken and of how a court characterised an ecological asset; the third is an assertion with no agency, no method and no baseline. None is a geomorphic measurement, and the programme's own stated method — native species on scrubland, wasteland and degraded forest across a six-million-hectare landscape, water-body rejuvenation, agroforestry and pasture — is area-based condition treatment rather than a line held against a frontier (Lesson 13 and Lesson 16). The relationship therefore holds as far as **what a claim can mean** and fails at **what a claim is evidence for**. A geographer can say what process is occurring, how it would be measured, and which measure matches which pathway. What the physics cannot supply is the rate of advance that does not exist, the aeolian share that was not obtained, or the recovery timescale that no source states.

**Misconception to avoid:** Do not conclude from this that policy language is careless or that programmes framed in frontier terms are misconceived. The correct inference is narrower and more useful: rationale and measurement are different registers, a candidate's task is to report both accurately and label which is which, and an answer that does so is more credible than one that endorses or ridicules either.

# ORIGINAL 10-, 15- AND 20-MARK MAINS MODEL PRACTICE

⚠️ These are **original practice prompts written for this edition**. ❗ They are **not previous-year questions and must not be cited as such.** ❗ None duplicates a lesson-local practice question. ⚠️ Every rubric is specific to its own answer, and every model is **word-counted, not estimated**.

## 10-mark practice (about 150 words)

**Practice question 1 [10] — mechanism and consequence.** "Wind erosion removes not a layer of soil but a fraction of it." Explain, and state why the distinction matters. *(Explain — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model answer (original, 145 words):** Wind sorts what it moves. Coarse sand and larger clasts cannot be lifted and remain as a lag that soon armours the surface; medium and fine sand hops in saltation within the region, building dunes; only silt and clay enter suspension, because their settling velocity falls below the upward turbulent eddies. What leaves is therefore not a uniform slice of the profile but its **finest fraction** — and that fraction carries the clay, the organic matter and most of the plant nutrients. The distinction matters for three reasons. Depth loss understates capability loss, since the land loses disproportionately what made it productive. The loss is irreversible at human timescales, because suspended dust travels thousands of kilometres and no process returns it. And the remaining armoured surface looks stable while being conditionally so, since breaking the lag exposes sheltered fines and restarts deflation at full rate.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 3 for the sorting argument distinguishing what stays from what leaves; 3 for identifying the exported fraction as the nutrient-bearing one and deriving capability loss from it; 2 for irreversibility at human timescales with a transport-distance justification; 2 for the conditional stability of the residual armour. Deduct 2 for describing wind erosion as uniform removal of a soil layer.

**Practice question 2 [10] — evidence and its limits.** Examine the claim that India's national land-degradation figure tells us how fast degradation is proceeding. *(Examine — answer in about 150 words.)*

**Model answer (original, 148 words):** The claim overreaches. The Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change reports 97.84 million hectares of land degradation and desertification for 2018-19, mapped by the Space Applications Centre, with a published technical guideline and a portal visualising degraded area together with its causal processes. That is a strong figure: it carries agency, period and a documented method, which textbook estimates of wind-eroded area conspicuously do not. But it is a **mapped condition for a stated period**, not a rate. The only change evidence is a two-epoch comparison between 2003-05 and 2018-19, and a comparison of two snapshots cannot describe the path between them, cannot be divided into an annual rate, and cannot by itself separate real change in land condition from change in classification or sensor between assessments. The qualification is that direction of change may still be stated, provided both epochs and the method caveat are given.

**Rubric (quantified, 10 marks):** 2 for the figure quoted with agency, period and reporting instrument; 3 for the strengths that distinguish it from undated textbook estimates; 3 for the mapped-condition-against-rate distinction argued rather than asserted; 2 for the method-change caveat on the two-epoch comparison. Deduct 3 for converting the comparison into an annual rate.

## 15-mark practice (about 250 words)

**Practice question 3 [15] — the origin dispute.** "The Thar is partly natural but largely man made." Critically examine this proposition, stating what evidence would be required to settle it. *(Critically examine — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original, 247 words):** The proposition bundles two questions that have different answers. The first is why western Rajasthan is dry. That is climatic: a subtropical position supplies a high evaporative demand, continentality contributes though less than for a true interior desert since the Arabian Sea is only a few hundred kilometres away, the summer monsoon current reaches the north-west but arrives with weak lifting so moisture is seasonally present while rain is not, and the Aravalli runs south-west to north-east along the flow rather than across it, offering little forced ascent. No human variable creates a monsoon margin. The second question is what condition the surface is in, and there the human contribution is substantial and mechanically explicable. Removing cover lowers the thresholds that govern entrainment; concentrated grazing breaks the coarse lag that armours a stony surface; tillage shatters cohesive aggregates. Khullar's own attributed causes — uncontrolled grazing, reckless felling and growing population — are real mechanisms. What the proposition asserts beyond this is an **apportionment**, and apportionment requires evidence of a kind not available: dated dune stratigraphy, palaeo-lake levels, pollen and phytolith sequences, archaeological occupation density, and a counterfactual. None appears in the sources consulted. The canonical owner meanwhile holds that people are not inherently a degradation driver and that drivers interact differently by site and timescale, and at a fluctuating monsoon margin surface condition varies with the rainfall of the year. The defensible formulation separates existence from condition: aridity's existence is climatic, its severity and productivity substantially human-modified.

**Rubric (quantified, 15 marks):** 4 for the two-question separation stated and used as the organising structure; 3 for at least three climatic controls named with what each does and does not explain; 3 for the human mechanisms given with their physical basis rather than asserted; 3 for naming the specific evidence classes that would be required to settle apportionment and recording their absence; 2 for the canonical caveat and the variability confound at a monsoon margin. Deduct 3 for asserting either determinist pole and 2 for any uncited chronology.

**Practice question 4 [15] — measurement and policy.** "In dryland geography, every boundary is a convention." Discuss with reference to desert extent, regional divisions and degradation classes. *(Discuss — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original, 250 words):** The proposition is defensible across all three. **Desert extent** varies with the criterion adopted: standard sources give the Thar as 260,000 square kilometres in one table, 259,000 in the same book's India extremes list, and about two lakh square kilometres in total with 1.75 lakh in India in another — differences arising from whether the Pakistani extension is counted, whether the semi-arid Bagar is included, and whether the eastern limit is taken as the Aravalli or the twenty-five centimetre isohyet. The advanced canonical owner anticipates this, holding that book estimates vary with the boundary used and should be retained as source-era figures. **Regional divisions** are explicitly scholarly decisions: India has been divided into three major natural regions, into three macro regions with thirty-four first-order, seventy-four second-order and two hundred and twenty-five subdivisions, and into seven divisions, by three different schemes on three different criteria. **Degradation classes** are conventions of a stricter kind, because they are operationally defined: desertification is land degradation restricted to arid, semi-arid and dry sub-humid areas, and where the dry sub-humid class ends depends on an aridity-index threshold that different conventions place differently. Even a judicial definition of the Aravalli works this way, adopting the uniform criterion that landforms rising a hundred metres above local relief are hills and that the range comprises landforms within five hundred metres of two such hills. The qualification is that a convention is not arbitrariness: an operational definition with a published method is checkable, which an undated textbook estimate is not.

**Rubric (quantified, 15 marks):** 3 for desert extent shown to vary with criterion, with at least three sourced figures and their differing scopes; 3 for regionalisation presented as competing scholarly schemes with their resolutions; 3 for degradation classes identified as operational definitions with a threshold dependence; 3 for a fourth independent instance, judicial or cartographic, reinforcing the finding; 3 for the closing qualification distinguishing a documented convention from arbitrariness. Deduct 3 for concluding that measurement is therefore unreliable.

## 20-mark practice (about 250 words)

**Practice question 5 [20] — the aeolian share against the other pathways.** "Desertification in India is primarily a problem of wind." Critically examine this proposition against the full range of degradation pathways. *(Critically examine — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original, 248 words):** The proposition is defensible for part of the arid zone and indefensible nationally. Wind's mechanism is real and severe. Deflation strips fines; saltation is self-amplifying because each landing grain ejects several more; abrasion is concentrated near the ground; suspension exports the clay, organic matter and nutrients beyond the region irrecoverably; and deposition buries cropland and remobilises dunes. In the western Marusthali, where mean dune heights reach about thirty metres at Barmer and dunes shift, wind is plainly dominant, and the advanced owner records that wind erosion drives more than forty per cent of Rajasthan's desertification. Nationally the picture changes. Dryland degradation runs through four pathways. Water erosion is tabulated separately: ravines cover 12.30 lakh hectares in Uttar Pradesh, 6.83 in Madhya Pradesh and 4.52 in Rajasthan. Salinity and alkalinity affect about 80 lakh hectares, some 2.43 per cent of India, led by Uttar Pradesh at 12.95 lakh hectares, with Rajasthan fifth at 7.28. Vegetation loss enables all the others. The source's own national soil-erosion map carries desert erosion as **one of six** mapped classes. Two states-level facts settle it: Uttar Pradesh leads both the ravine and the salinity tables, and Rajasthan leads neither. The honest limitation is that the aeolian share of India's reported 97.84 million hectares for 2018-19 is not publicly reconstructable from the sources used here, so the argument is structural rather than quantitative. The defensible verdict is that wind is the dominant pathway in a defined subregion and one pathway among four at national scale.

**Rubric (quantified, 20 marks):** 5 for the aeolian mechanism set out as a causal chain with at least four stages and the self-amplification or export argument; 5 for all four pathways identified and at least two supported with state-resolved sourced figures; 4 for the decisive comparison that the leading state differs by pathway; 3 for the scale distinction between subregional dominance and national proportion; 3 for the explicit limitation that the aeolian share is unavailable, stated rather than filled with an estimate. Deduct 4 for asserting national aeolian dominance and 3 for any invented share.

**Practice question 6 [20] — rationale against measurement.** "Public action against desertification is framed in the language of a moving frontier, while the science describes a change in place." Critically examine this tension and assess how an answer should handle it. *(Critically examine — answer in about 250 words.)*

**Model answer (original, 249 words):** Both halves of the proposition are accurate and the tension is real, dated and resolvable. The frontier language is genuine and first-party. The Aravalli Green Wall Project, launched on 25 March 2023, lists among its objectives preventing the eastward expansion of the Thar and reducing land degradation by creating green barriers. A government backgrounder of 21 December 2025 records the Supreme Court emphasising the range's ecological importance as a barrier against desertification, a groundwater recharge zone and a biodiversity habitat. A standard textbook separately asserts advance at about half a kilometre a year. The scientific description differs in kind. Desertification is land degradation restricted to arid, semi-arid and dry sub-humid areas; degradation is loss of productivity of a place, which has no direction of travel. India's own national assessment accordingly reports degraded area by condition class, state and causal process for a mapped period rather than tracking a boundary. The resolution is to distinguish registers. The programme and judicial statements are rationales for public action, not geomorphic measurements, and neither document supplies a rate; the textbook rate carries no agency, method or baseline. Conversely the imagery captures something true at local scale, since mobile sand does encroach on cultivated land at a desert margin. The programme's own method confirms the reading: native species on scrubland, wasteland and degraded forest across a six-million-hectare landscape, water-body rejuvenation, agroforestry and pasture is area-based condition treatment. An answer should report both registers accurately, label which is which, and neither endorse nor ridicule either.

**Rubric (quantified, 20 marks):** 4 for the policy and judicial statements reported accurately with their dates; 4 for the scientific description of degradation as in-place capability loss, with the definitional reason it has no direction; 4 for the register distinction stated as the resolution; 3 for conceding what the imagery gets right at local scale; 3 for the programme's own stated method used as corroborating evidence; 2 for the closing instruction to label rather than to endorse or ridicule. Deduct 4 for treating a programme objective as a scientific finding and 3 for dismissing the programme as misconceived.

# REMEDIATION

⚠️ Twenty-two errors that recur when this topic is used in an answer, each with the reason it fails, the repair, and the lesson that prevents it. ❗ The first six target the normalisation items most likely to be reproduced from memory: the advancing desert, the man-made origin, the latitude band and the insolation account.

| # | Error | Why it fails | Repair | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | *"The Thar is advancing at about half a kilometre a year"* | ❗ The figure carries **no agency, no method, no baseline and no statement of what advances** — a sand surface, a climatic class or a degraded-land condition class are three different things | *"Degradation is a loss of land capability in place, reported in condition classes; mobile sand encroaches locally but the climatic zone does not march"* | **L13**, **L15** |
| **R2** | Treating the Aravalli Green Wall's objective or the Court's framing as proof that the desert is advancing | ❗ **A programme rationale and a judicial observation are statements of why action was taken, not measurements**, and neither document supplies a rate | *"Report both registers and label which is which; the programme's own method is area-based condition treatment across a six-million-hectare landscape"* | **L13**, **L16** |
| **R3** | *"The Rajasthan desert is largely man made"* | ❗ It is an **apportionment with no apportionment evidence** — no dated dune ages, no pollen sequence, no occupation series, no counterfactual in any source here | *"Aridity's existence is a climatic outcome; its severity, surface condition and productivity are substantially human-modified"* | **L13** |
| **R4** | *"Almost all deserts lie between fifteen and thirty degrees"* | ❗ It describes **one route to aridity** and makes the standing trap that the largest deserts are cold unanswerable | *"Subtropical subsidence explains the trade-wind family; continentality, rain shadow, cold currents and polar cold place the rest outside the band"* | **L1** |
| **R5** | Explaining desert rock breakdown by day-night heating alone | ❗ **Insolation cycling alone does not disintegrate rock**; moisture is required, and salt weathering is the characteristic arid mechanism | *"Moisture is required; salt weathering dominates many arid settings because evaporation concentrates the salts that do the work"* — then route the mechanics to the weathering owner | **L3** |
| **R6** | Using *desert* and *desertification* interchangeably | ❗ The examiner's own question uses both terms separately; a desert **cannot** desertify, because the arid core has no productivity left to lose | *"Desertification is land degradation in arid, semi-arid and dry sub-humid areas; the land at risk is the productive margin"* | **L15** |
| **R7** | Defining a desert by heat or by sand | ❗ Defeats Antarctica, the Atacama, the Namib and the reg-and-hamada majority of desert surfaces | *"A desert is a water-balance deficit: potential evapotranspiration greatly exceeds precipitation"* | **L1**, **L2** |
| **R8** | *"Deserts are mostly sand seas"* | ❗ The erg requires **three conditions at once** — supply, energy and an accumulating basin; the source's own ordering is *"some rocky, others stony and the rest sandy"* | *"Most desert surfaces are rock or gravel; reg is a residue and hamada a swept surface"* | **L2** |
| **R9** | Attributing aeolian landscapes to exceptionally strong desert winds | ❗ Wind speed is atmospheric; **every limiting condition is a property of the surface** | *"Aeolian work is limited by dry, loose, uncohesive material on an unprotected surface, not by wind strength"* | **L3** |
| **R10** | *"Finer material is always easier for wind to lift"* | ❗ The entrainment threshold is lowest for fine and medium **sand** and rises again for silt and clay because of **cohesion** | *"Dust is usually ejected by saltating sand striking a cohesive surface, not lifted directly"* | **L4** |
| **R11** | Listing creep, saltation and suspension as three equivalent modes | ❗ They differ in grain size, trajectory, distance and geomorphic job; **suspension carries most of the mass, saltation does most of the work** | *"Saltation is the engine — it abrades, it drives creep, it ejects the dust — while suspension is the export route"* | **L4** |
| **R12** | Merging deflation and abrasion | ❗ Deflation **removes**; abrasion **wears down** and requires a carried tool | *"A hole or a lowering is deflation; a shape cut into standing rock is abrasion"* | **L5** |
| **R13** | Calling mesas, buttes or inselbergs wind-eroded landforms | ❗ They are **structurally controlled residuals of regional denudation**; the canonical owner denies the wind-only attribution explicitly | *"The plain was lowered around a resistant mass; a mesa's flat top is a bedding surface, not an erosion surface"* | **L5** |
| **R14** | *"Barchan horns point into the wind"* / *"parabolic horns point downwind"* | ❗ Both inverted. Barchan horns trail **downwind** because thin margins outrun the bulky centre; parabolic horns point **upwind** because vegetation anchors them | *"Same crescent, opposite orientation: the difference is whether vegetation is holding the tips"* | **L6** |
| **R15** | Transferring a textbook dune-advance rate to the Thar | ❗ Twenty-five and fifty feet a year are **source-era illustrative figures in imperial units** for unnamed dunes; they are not an Indian measurement and not evidence of desert advance | *"Quote them, if at all, as a mid-century illustration of dune mobility and never as an Indian figure"* | **L6**, **L13** |
| **R16** | *"Loess is a water-laid silt"* | ❗ Loess is **non-stratified**, because it settles from air; a water-laid deposit is almost always bedded | *"Absence of stratification plus a blanket relationship to topography is the field test"* | **L7** |
| **R17** | *"The Thar is shaped almost entirely by wind, as its dry riverbeds show"* | ❗ A steep-sided dry channel is **positive evidence of water**; the canonical owner holds that episodic water does more to shape most desert landscapes than wind | *"The distinctive suite — wadi, fan, bajada, pediment, playa — is cut and built by episodic runoff, and water delivers the sediment wind later sorts"* | **L8** |
| **R18** | Confusing bajada with pediment | ❗ Same position in the profile, **opposite origin**: one is a depositional apron of alluvial material, the other an erosional rock-cut plain | *"Bedrock beneath means pediment; unconsolidated gravel beneath means bajada. Slope angle decides nothing"* | **L8** |
| **R19** | Treating desert pavement as a landform, or as evidence that erosion has ceased | ❗ It is a **self-limiting feedback**: the lag it leaves shelters the fines beneath, and its stability is conditional on remaining continuous | *"Break the lag and deflation restarts at full rate, commonly opening a self-deepening blow-out"* | **L9** |
| **R20** | Giving one Thar area, or treating the national degraded-land figure as a Thar or wind figure | ❗ Four Thar figures exist on four pages with four different scopes; the national figure covers **all degradation processes** for **2018-19** | *"State a figure with its source, page and boundary, or state the range and why it varies"* | **L10**, **L14** |
| **R21** | Quoting 45 million hectares as India's severely wind-eroded area | ❗ The same folio also says 21 million hectares, for the same class, citing the same figure, with the state list differing only by one addition; **neither carries a method** | *"Standard textbook estimates are internally inconsistent; use the Ministry-reported 97.84 million hectares for 2018-19 on the Space Applications Centre's mapping"* | **L14** |
| **R22** | Listing restoration measures without attaching a pathway, or summing the three restoration instruments | ❗ A pathway-blind list could be written about anywhere; and a Bonn Challenge pledge, a neutrality target and a Twenty Point Programme afforestation area have **three different baselines and reporting bodies** | *"Match the measure to the pathway; and report the instruments separately, since no fetched document reconciles them"* | **L16** |

## Map 3 — Pediment against bajada, and playa against the wider desert

❗ These are the two distinctions the canonical terminology control names explicitly, and both are decided by **origin**, not by appearance.

```text
   DISTINCTION 1 - SAME PLACE, OPPOSITE ORIGIN

   mountain scarp
        |\
        | \  . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .
        |  \ . . . . .  B A J A D A  . . . . . . .    DEPOSITIONAL
        |   \. . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .  "a depositional
        |____\___________________________              feature made up
        |     \________                                of alluvial
        |  P E D I M E N T \_________                  material laid
        |  "an EROSIONAL plain formed at the           down by the
        |   base of the surrounding mountain           intermittent
        |   scarps"                                    streams"
        +------------------------------------------>

   THE TEST:  dig.  BEDROCK continuous with the mountain -> PEDIMENT
                    UNCONSOLIDATED gravel/sand/scree    -> BAJADA
   NOT the test:  slope angle (both gentle) | position (same) |
                  surface appearance (both debris-veneered)
   WHY IT MATTERS: a bajada is a SEDIMENT STORE that wind can
   re-entrain (L3 rung 1); a pediment is an EROSIONAL RECORD of
   long-continued scarp retreat.

   -------------------------------------------------------------

   DISTINCTION 2 - PLAYA AGAINST THE WIDER DESERT

   +--------------------------------------------------------------+
   |  THE WIDER DESERT                                            |
   |  - defined by a CLIMATIC water-balance deficit                |
   |  - surface: erg | reg | hamada | badlands | mountain          |
   |  - dominant agents: wind AND episodic runoff                  |
   |  - salt: dispersed, in soil accumulation horizons             |
   |  - extent: regional, hundreds of km                           |
   |                                                              |
   |     +------------------------------------------------+       |
   |     | THE PLAYA                                      |       |
   |     | - defined by a DRAINAGE condition: a CLOSED    |       |
   |     |   BASIN with no outlet to the sea              |       |
   |     | - surface: lake-bed sediment + EVAPORITE crust |       |
   |     | - dominant agent: EVAPORATIVE CONCENTRATION    |       |
   |     | - salt: CONCENTRATED, commercially extractable |       |
   |     | - extent: a basin floor, tens of km            |       |
   |     | - AREA oscillates seasonally; SALT MASS does   |       |
   |     |   not fall                                     |       |
   |     | - regional names: playa | salina | salar |     |       |
   |     |   shott                                        |       |
   |     +------------------------------------------------+       |
   +--------------------------------------------------------------+

   THE ERROR THIS PREVENTS: treating a shrinking playa as evidence of
   desiccation. Seasonal contraction is its NORMAL behaviour -
   Sambhar occupies about 225 sq km in the rains and shrinks
   considerably in the dry season, every year.
```
