# COMPLETE CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

⚠️ Compressed revision notes covering all eighteen lessons. ❗ They do not repeat the teaching prose; they carry definitions, mechanisms, sourced figures with their pages, corrections, traps and answer routes.

## A. Aridity and desert surfaces — the enabling conditions (L1-L2)

- ⚠️ **Aridity = P much less than PET.** A water-balance deficit. ❗ Not heat, not sand, not distance from the sea.
- ⚠️ **Five routes:** subtropical subsidence (Sahara, Arabian, Kalahari, Australian) · continentality (Gobi, Turkestan) · rain shadow (Patagonia) · cold offshore current (Atacama, Namib — cool, foggy, extremely dry) · polar aridity (Antarctica). ❗ Independent, and usually **more than one operates**.
- ❌ **[N5]** ✅ Leong: *"almost all the deserts are confined within the 15° to 30° parallels"* — ❗ describes **one route**. ✅ Majid: *"Antarctica, is a cold desert."*
- ✅ **Scale anchors:** Leong — *"About a fifth of the world's land is made up of deserts, some rocky, others stony and the rest sandy."* Majid Table 6.30 — lowest average annual precipitation, **Quillagua 0.05 cm**. ❗ Source-era tabulated extreme.
- ✅ **Regime, once only, source-era imperial:** rainfall often **under 5 inches** (≈125 mm), mean temperature about **87 °F** (≈31 °C). 🔁 Topic 18 owns the regime.
- 🇮🇳 ✅ **Khullar, folio 212:** India **12.13 %** arid, *"about 30 per cent"* semi-arid; NW semi-aridity from **distance from the sea**, southern from the **Western Ghats rain shadow**; **Leh and Ladakh** cold desert, rainfall *"hardly exceeds 20 cm."* ❗ India shows **three of five** routes; **no trade-wind desert, no cold current**.
- ⚠️ **Owner's three surfaces:** **erg** (needs supply + energy + a basin, all three) · **reg / serir** (a **residue**; a coarse lag that **armours**) · **hamada** (bare rock).
- ✅ **Leong's five landscape types**, adding **badlands** (South Dakota; Painted Desert of Arizona, *"south-east of the Grand Canyon"*) and **mountain deserts** (Ahaggar, Tibesti). ✅ **Hamada el Homra**, Libya, *"almost 20,000 square miles"*. ✅ **Calanscio Sand Sea**; Turkestan **koum**; ✅ Majid — largest sand seas in Saudi Arabia, the **Rub-al-Khali**.
- ❗ **The sorting mechanism:** silt and clay → suspension → **loess**; sand → dunes → **erg**; gravel and clods → lag → **reg**; all loose material swept → **hamada**.
- 🇮🇳 ❗ **India has no world-scale erg.** ✅ Khullar: eastern Marusthali **rocky**, western covered by **dhrian**; bedrock outcrops *"prove that geologically it is a part of the Peninsular Plateau."*

## B. Supply, entrainment and transport — the engine (L3-L4)

- ⚠️ **The supply ladder, bottom to top:** detached material of liftable size → exposed surface → dry and uncohesive → room to move. ❗ All four or nothing happens. ❗ **Wind speed is atmospheric; every rung is a property of the surface.**
- ✅ **Leong's thesis sentence:** wind is *"not the most effective agent of erosion, transportation and deposition"* but *"is more efficient in arid than in humid regions"* because there is *"little vegetation or moisture to bind the loose surface materials."*
- ✅ **Leong on weathering's role:** the fragments *"become the 'teeth' or tools of wind erosion."*
- ⚠️ **Vegetation protects three ways:** canopy **absorbs** momentum · roots **bind** · litter **covers**. 🔑 **Absorb, bind, cover.**
- ❗ **Moisture is a binder; dry clay is cohesive.** Tillage converts a cohesive surface into an erodible one with no change in climate.
- ❌ **[N6]** ✅ Leong's *"onion-peeling"* exfoliation from heating and cooling alone is **superseded**; **moisture is required** and **salt weathering** dominates many arid settings. 🔁 Topic 04 owns the mechanics. ✅ His frost note, *"water expands 10 per cent"*, stands for cold deserts.
- ⚠️ **Supply-limited** (bare rock; add sediment) against **transport-limited** (broad erg; add wind). ❗ **Human amplification converts a protected or supply-limited surface into a transport-limited one.**
- ✅ **Three erosion processes (Majid):** **attrition** (grains wear each other) · **deflation** (*"removes fine material from the surface"*) · **abrasion / corrasion** (*"wearing away by friction as by windborne sand"*). ❗ Deflation **takes away**; abrasion **wears down** and needs a **tool**.
- ⚠️ **Two thresholds:** **fluid** starts transport; the lower **impact** threshold sustains it. ⚠️ Threshold is lowest for fine and medium sand, higher for coarse (weight) and higher again for silt and clay (**cohesion**). ❗ **Dust is usually ejected, not lifted.**
- ✅ **Khullar's size classes:** coarse sand **1.0-2.0 mm**, gravels, peds and clods → **desert pavement**; **saltation** = very fine, fine and medium sand; **creep** = coarse sand. ✅ Majid: *"Most of the load is carried by wind in suspension."*
- ⚠️ **The cascade:** lift → forward momentum → low-angle impact → **several grains ejected** → multiplication → saturation. ❗ **Self-amplifying**; also drives creep and generates suspension. ⚠️ **Suspension carries most of the mass; saltation does most of the work.**
- ✅ **Leong:** dust travels *"as far as 2,300 miles"*; Saharan dust falls as **"blood rains"** in Italy and on Swiss glaciers; the **Great Dust Bowl** followed land *"stripped of their natural vegetation for farming."* ✅ Majid: **haboobs** of Sudan, Egypt, Saudi Arabia and Yemen; wind-blown dust covers *"about one-tenth of the land surface"*, yielding *"some of the richest soils of the Earth."*
- ⚠️ **Wind against water:** wind is **not channel-confined**, so it moves material **uphill and across divides**, and its fine fraction **leaves the region**. ✅ Khullar: blown sand lands on *"adjoining fertile lands whose fertility dwindles."*

## C. Wind-erosion and depositional landforms (L5-L7)

- ❗ **Diagnostic:** a hole or lowering → **deflation**; a shape cut into standing rock → **abrasion**.
- ✅ **Deflation hollows:** **Qattara** *"almost 450 feet below sea level"* (≈137 m); **Faiyum, Egypt**, *"130 feet"* (≈40 m). ❗ **The floor is the WATER TABLE** — ✅ *"Water then seeps out forming oases or swamps"*; moisture binds, deflation stops.
- ✅ **Desert pavement:** ✅ Majid — fines removed from *"the mountain wash"*, *"leaving a mosaic of pebbles"*; ✅ Leong — faceted pebbles form *"a smooth, mosaic-like region."* ❗ The raw material is **water-delivered**.
- ✅ **Majid's erosional inventory:** blowouts/deflation basins · bowls and caves · **demoiselles** · desert pavements · **dreikanter** · inselberg · **ventifacts** · wind bridges (wind windows) · **yardangs** · **zeugens**.
- ✅ **Yardang:** long axis **parallel** to the wind; ratio **3:1 or greater**; height *"few metres to 200"* m; **U-shaped or flat-bottomed troughs**. ✅ **Zeugen:** *"tabular mass of rock perched on a pinnacle of a softer rock."* ✅ **Demoiselle:** *"A pillar of Earth… protected from a capping boulder."* ✅ **Dreikanter:** **three** facets — a record of **wind-direction change**.
- ✅ **Leong's control observation:** abrasion *"is most effective at or near the base of rocks… This explains why telegraph poles in the deserts are protected by a covering of metal for a foot or two above the ground."* ❗ The **height-zonation** that generates the pedestal-and-cap family.
- ❌ **[N8]** the scan's *"hornhardts"* is an optical error for **bornhardt**. ✅ **Uluru / Ayers Rock** named. ❌ **Mesas, buttes and inselbergs are denudational residuals, not wind forms** — ✅ Leong: inselbergs are *"probably the relics of an original plateau which has been almost entirely eroded away."*
- ❗ **Source defect:** the Leong page carrying rock pedestals and zeugen, yardangs, and mesas and buttes is a **blank scan**. ❗ **No Leong citation is attached to that material anywhere in this file.**
- ✅ **Dunes (Leong):** *"hills of sand… shaped by the movement of winds"*, **active or 'live'** against **inactive fixed dunes, rooted with vegetation**. ⚠️ **Four-fold scheme (Majid):** longitudinal (seif), transverse, parabolic, star — plus the **barchan** as the supply-limited transverse case. ✅ Size *"less than a metre to over 200 m high."*
- ✅ **Barchan:** *"crescentic or moon-shaped"*; horns *"thin out and become lower in the direction of the wind due to the reduced frictional retardation of the winds around the edges"*; windward **convex and gently-sloping**, leeward **concave and steep (the slip-face)**; initiated *"by a chance accumulation of sand at an obstacle"*; migrates as sand is *"driven up the windward side and… slips down the leeward side."* ✅ Rates **25 ft/yr** (dunes to 100 ft) and **50 ft/yr** (low dunes). ❗ **Small dunes move faster.** ❗ **Never transferred to India.**
- ✅ **Sand-supply control (Majid):** barchans form *"where either sand transport rates are high and/or sand supply is limited"* and *"frequently occur on the margins of sand seas and migrate over non-sandy surfaces."* ❗ **A bare inter-dune floor is the diagnostic.**
- ✅ **Seif:** Arabic *"sword"*; *"often over a hundred miles long lying parallel to the direction of the prevailing winds"*; *"over 200 feet"*; crestline *"like the teeth of a monstrous saw"*; corridors *"swept clear of sand"*; dominant wind **lengthens**, cross winds **thicken**; named in the **Sahara south of the Qattara Depression, the Thar Desert and the West Australian Desert**. ⚠️ Modern account adds a **bimodal or seasonally shifting** regime.
- ⚠️ **Parabolic:** horns point **UPWIND**, anchored by **vegetation**, originating in a **blow-out** — ❗ a degradation signature. ⚠️ **Star:** multidirectional wind plus abundant sand; **grows upward rather than migrating**.
- ⚠️ **Loess:** ✅ Majid — *"unconsolidated, non-stratified soil composed primarily of silt-sized particles… very fertile"*; originally a *"loose, fine and sharp-grained soil occurring in the Rhine Valley"*. ❗ **NON-STRATIFIED is the field test.** ✅ Leong — *"yellow, friable… rich in lime, very coherent and extremely porous"*, so it stands in near-vertical walls yet gullies rapidly.
- ✅ **Distribution (Majid):** Hwang-Ho basin · Mississippi valley · Patagonia · New Zealand · Tunisia · Negev · Central Europe · Central Asia · China · **Pakistan**. ⚠️ **Two families:** **desert-marginal** and **ice-marginal**. ❌ **[N7]** **hwangtu** and **limon** remain usable; **"adobe" for Mid-West loess is no longer standard** — ⚠️ reported, not quoted, because that column is **clipped in the scan**.
- 🇮🇳 ❗ **India has no loess plateau.** ✅ The analogue is **bhur** — Khullar, folio 76: *"an elevated piece of land situated along the banks of the Ganga river especially in the upper Ganga-Yamuna Doab… formed due to accumulation of wind-blown sands during the hot dry months of the year."* ❗ **Bhur is sand, not silt; it is not loess.** ❗ Indo-Gangetic fertility is **alluvial**.

## D. Water in deserts, and desert soils (L8-L9)

- ⚠️ **Owner's claim:** *"Over geological time, episodic water does more to shape most desert landscapes than wind does."*
- ⚠️ **The flood regime:** rare but intense rain → low infiltration on crusted, sparsely vegetated ground → rapid concentrated runoff → very high competence while it lasts. ⚠️ Flows so laden they become **liquid mud**; gullies and ravines → **badland topography**. ❗ **Desert work is done in rare high-magnitude events.**
- ✅ **Khullar's tempo line:** *"It is not the total annual rainfall that is important, but rather how and when it comes."*
- ⚠️ **The chain:** **wadi → alluvial fan ("dry delta") → bajada → pediment → playa**, with **inselbergs** above the pediment plain. ⚠️ Fan mechanism = **loss of confinement** → competence collapses → coarse at apex, fining outward.
- ✅ **Bajada (Majid):** *"the gentle, sloping surface leading down from a mountain front to inland basins"*, of *"sand, gravel, and angular scree"*, mantling *"the underlying rock-cut pediment."* ✅ **Pediment (Leong):** *"an erosional plain formed at the base of the surrounding mountain scarps."* ✅ Majid attributes the term to **G.K. Gilbert (1880), Utah** — ❗ his equation of it with alluvial fans **conflates** the two and is not used.
- ❗ **The test:** bedrock beneath → **pediment**; unconsolidated gravel → **bajada**. ❗ Slope angle decides nothing.
- ✅ **Playa (Leong):** *"drainage is almost entirely internal"*; lakes *"contain a high percentage of salts… and are glistening white when they dry up"*; called **playas, salinas or salars** and **shotts**.
- ❗ **The salt argument:** inputs carry solutes · no outlet · **vapour carries no solutes** · therefore salt accumulates. ❗ Makes **salinisation latent** if the water table is later raised.
- ⚠️ **Exotic streams:** fed by snowmelt outside the desert; owner names the **Nile and Colorado**; Algerian gorges called **chebka**.
- ⚠️ **Arid soils:** net flux is **upward**, so solutes **accumulate** rather than leach. ⚠️ Mineral- and base-rich · organic-poor (low input **and** fast oxidation) · alkaline with **carbonate, then gypsum, then soluble salts** ordered by solubility · shallow and weakly horizonated. ❗ **Chemically fertile, structurally fragile.**
- ✅ **Formal name:** **Aridisol**, with **reddish desert**, **gray desert** and **sierozem**; *"limited alteration of parent material, low climate activity, light colour."* ❗ The numerical figure in that row is **not quoted**, because its base is not established.
- ❗ **[N8]** the Indian soils table at printed p.10.34 carries **two rows both labelled desert soil**; ❗ **no row from it is used and no Indian desert-soil area is asserted.** Source line: **Chatterji, S.P., 1973 and 1986**.
- ❗ **Pavement is a FEEDBACK:** fines removed → lag concentrates → continuous → fines sheltered → deflation stops. ❗ **Break the lag → blow-out → full-rate deflation**, and the hollow funnels wind and deepens itself. ⚠️ A blow-out in a **vegetated** sand surface becomes a **parabolic dune**.
- ❗ **Sodicity worsens both erosion pathways:** dispersion → crust → less infiltration → more runoff; break the crust → structureless fines → more wind erosion.

## E. The Thar — location, mosaic and drainage (L10-L12)

- ⚠️ **Four contributions, none sufficient:** subtropical position (high **PET**) · continentality (✅ *"long distances from the sea"*, ❗ weaker here than for the Gobi) · **monsoon geometry — weak lifting** · Aravalli alignment **along** the flow. ❗ **Moisture present, rain absent.** ❗ No single mechanism for the weak lifting is asserted.
- ❌ **[N4] Four areas, four pages:** ✅ **260,000 sq km** (Majid, printed 6.48, Table 6.31) · ✅ **259,000 sq km** (Majid, printed 10.5) · ✅ **~2.0 lakh sq km total** and ✅ **1.75 lakh sq km in India** (Khullar, folio 76). ❗ A **boundary-definition artefact**. ✅ Advanced owner: *"Book estimates of desert area vary with the boundary used."* ✅ Majid's Asian list: *"Thar-Cholistan Desert: India and Pakistan."*
- ✅ **Position:** *"the western extremity of the Great Plain of India"*; **650 km long, 250-300 km wide**; **two-thirds in Rajasthan west of the Aravali**, one-third in **Haryana, Punjab and Gujarat**.
- ✅ **Elevations:** overall average **~325 m**; descending to **~150 m** near the Indo-Pak border and toward the Indus Valley and the Rann; **Marusthali 200-250 m**; East Rajasthan Upland **250-500 m**.
- ✅ **The mosaic:** **Marusthali** (desert proper; eastern part rocky on Peninsular bedrock, western part **dhrian**) · **thali** (*"the tract north of the Luni"*) · **Rajasthan Bagar** (semi-arid, from the **Aravalli edge** east to the **25 cm isohyet** west, NE-SW, with fertile **rohi** patches, drained by *"short seasonal streams originating from the Aravali"*).
- ✅ **Regionalisation history:** **L.D. Stamp 1922** (3 major regions, 22 sub-regions) · **J.N.L. Baker 1928** · **O.H.K. Spate 1954**, revised **1967** (3 macro, **34 first-order excluding islands, 74 second-order, 225 subdivisions**; macro regions = mountain rim, Indo-Gangetic plain, Peninsula) · **S.P. Chatterjee 1965**, *Gazetteer of India* Vol. I (**seven divisions**).
- ✅ **Khullar folio 92:** *"The Western plain consists of arid land of Rajasthan. It has a well defined boundary in the east marked by the Aravalli range. This is a sandy desert with distinct aeolian topography. The eastern part is less sandy, comparatively more humid and is clothed with steppe vegetation."*
- ✅ **Six LANDSAT dune types:** obstacle · parabolic · longitudinal · transverse · barchan · shrub-coppice. ❗ **Obstacle and shrub-coppice forms signal vegetation present.** ✅ Mean height **30 m at Barmer** → **8 m at Jaipur and Sikar**; lengths **5-10 km**.
- ❗ **Four gradients move together west to east:** elevation up · rainfall up · dune height down · vegetation up.
- ✅ **Drainage:** the **Luni**, *"a seasonal stream which flows towards the south-west to the Rann of Kachchh"*; ✅ map bank — *"Does not normally reach the Arabian Sea as a perennial river."* ✅ *"North of the Luni basin, there is a large area of inland drainage… having several saline lakes"* — **Sambhar, Didwana, Degana, Kuchaman, Sargol, Khatu**, *"some of the important lakes."*
- ✅ **Sambhar:** **65 km west of Jaipur**, altitude **~360 m** *"in the Aravali terrain"*, **30 km long**, width **3-8 km**, *"about 225 sq km during the rainy season but shrinks considerably in dry season."* ❗ A **playa in all but name**.
- ❌ **Rann correction, mandatory:** ✅ advanced owner — the Rann is *"a distinct saline mudflat/marsh system, not simply another field of Thar dunes"*, though ✅ Khullar *"treats the Rann of Kachchh as an EXTENSION of the Thar desert."* ✅ Map bank — *"Not a conventional sand desert"*; *"Great Rann is a salt marsh."* ❗ **Regionally linked, geomorphically distinct.**
- ✅ **National salinity (Khullar):** about **80 lakh ha ≈ 2.43 % of India**; four settings — canal-irrigated · arid · semi-arid · coastal; **western Uttar Pradesh worst**. ✅ Vocabulary: **reh, kallar, usar, thur, rakar, karl, chopan**.

## F. Origin, evidence and definitions (L13-L15)

- ❗ **Two questions, never merged:** **(A)** why is it dry — **climatic**; **(B)** what condition is the surface in — **substantially human**. ❗ **Resolution:** *aridity's existence is a climatic outcome; its severity, surface condition and productivity are substantially human-modified.*
- ❓ **[N2]** ✅ Khullar, folio 212: *"The Rajasthan desert is partly natural but largely man made and the fear is often expressed, quite rightly too, that the desert is spreading."* ❗ *"Largely"* is an apportionment with **no apportionment evidence at any time depth**. ✅ Causes: *"uncontrolled grazing, reckless felling of trees and growing population. Climate change have also contributed."*
- ⚠️ **Causal control:** *"wind is not the sole geomorphic agent and people are not inherently a degradation driver."*
- ❌ **[N1] Four statements, all reported:** ✅ Khullar — *"advancing at an alarming rate of about 0.5 km per year"*; ⚠️ Basic owner — *"'advancing desert' imagery is misleading"*, degradation *"often recoverable"*; 📰 CA-3 (25 Mar 2023) — *"To prevent eastward expansion of Thar Desert"*; 📰 CA-4 (21 Dec 2025) — the Court on the Aravalli *"as a barrier against desertification."* ❗ **Rationale is not measurement.** ❗ **The rate is not repeated as fact.**
- ❗ **[N13]** the Aravalli is a **topographic and ecological transition** at the **eastern limit of continuous dune cover**; real functions are **local wind reduction, sand trapping, seasonal stream supply, groundwater recharge**. ❗ Not an orographic barrier, not a wall.
- ⚠️ **Determinism specimen, attributed, used once:** ✅ Majid, *Geography of India*, printed 2.28 — *"Had there been no Himalayas, the whole of northern India would have been a desert."* ❗ Single sufficient cause · untestable counterfactual · influence slid into determination.
- ❗ **[N3] The contradiction, both halves, never harmonised:** ✅ *"About 21 million hectares are subject to severe wind erosion in Rajasthan and adjoining areas of Punjab, Haryana, and Gujarat"* and ✅ *"About 45 million hectares of land is subject to severe wind erosion in Rajasthan and adjoining areas of Punjab, Haryana, Gujarat and Western Uttar Pradesh"* — same folio, same figure reference, both *"severe"*, no method on either.
- ❗ **The four-part test for any hectare figure:** **definition · scope · method · period.**
- ✅ **Other Khullar figures, each unmethodised:** *"over 80 million hectares or about one-fourth of our total area"* exposed to wind and water erosion; *"40 million hectares"* seriously eroded; *"about 40,000 hectares"* permanently lost; *"34 lakh tonnes"* removed yearly in **Jodhpur, Bikaner, Kota, Jaipur, Bharatpur, Kishangarh**. ❗ **[U10]** the *"8 million tonnes… from every square kilometre… during the last hundred years"* line is **not quoted as fact**.
- ✅ **Table 7.3 (per cent):** Rajasthan **57.42 / 36.67** · Gujarat **33.72 / 47.50** · Haryana **29.32 / 59.77** · Punjab **24.60 / 75.40** · Maharashtra **0.42 / 61.17** · AP and Telangana **7.18 / 44.66** · Karnataka **4.27 / 72.60** · Jammu and Kashmir **31.13 / 6.22** · UP **— / 21.73** · MP **— / 13.38** · Tamil Nadu **— / 15.54** · **Total average 12.13 / 29.13**.
- ❗ **[N12]** prose says *"about 30 per cent"*; **Table 7.3 says 29.13**. Quote the table with its number.
- ✅ **Table 7.1 ravines (lakh ha):** UP **12.30** · MP **6.83** · Rajasthan **4.52** · Gujarat **4.00** · Punjab **1.20** · West Bengal **1.04** · Bihar **0.60** · Tamil Nadu **0.60** · Maharashtra **0.20**.
- ✅ **Table 7.2 salinity (lakh ha):** UP **12.95** · Punjab **12.25** · Gujarat **12.14** · West Bengal **8.50** · Rajasthan **7.28** · Maharashtra **5.34** · Haryana **5.26** · Karnataka **4.04** · Odisha **4.04** · MP **2.24** · AP and Telangana **0.42** · Delhi **0.11** · Bihar **0.04** · Tamil Nadu **0.04**.
- ❗ **The limiting argument in Indian numbers:** **Uttar Pradesh leads both the ravine and the salinity tables; Rajasthan leads neither.**
- ✅ **Fig. 7.2 legend:** gully and sheet erosion · sheet erosion · **desert erosion (aeolian)** · shifting-cultivation erosion · glacial erosion · coastal erosion, plus a dashed **"LIMIT OF WIND EROSION"**. ❗ **Aeolian is one of six.** ❗ A limit line is **not a frontier line**. ✅ Also **Fig. 7.3 Ravines**, **Fig. 7.4 Saline and Alkaline Lands**.
- ❗ **[N9] 97.84 Mha controls.** 📰 ✅ PIB **1946401, 07 Aug 2023**: *"97.84 million hectares in 2018-19"*, SAC-ISRO atlas, with an **online portal** visualising degraded area *"with the processes causing degradation."* ⚠️ The advanced owner's **97.85** is the **same assessment**, not a second finding; its **29.7 %** and **28.76 %** are retained **only** with the note that they were **not obtained first-party**, and the **17 Jun 2021** release states **no national figure at all**. ❗ The atlas publication was **not downloaded**.
- ❗ **Mapped condition ≠ trend.** The **2003-05 → 2018-19** change analysis is a **two-epoch comparison**; ❗ **no annual rate anywhere in this file.**
- ⚠️ **Five terms:** **desert** = a zone · **aridity** = a state · **drought** = an event · **land degradation** = productivity loss in any climate · **desertification** = land degradation **restricted to drylands**.
- ✅ **Drought (Khullar, folio 237):** *"a temporary reduction in water or moisture availability significantly below the normal or expected amount for a specific period"*; ✅ **High Powered Committee on Disaster Management** — *"Any lack of water to satisfy the normal needs of agriculture, livestock, industry or human population may be termed as a drought."*
- ❗ **The decisive sentence:** *"A desert cannot 'desertify'; the land at risk is the productive dryland margin around it."*
- ⚠️ **Four pathways:** **aeolian · hydric · chemical · vegetative.** ❗ Vegetation loss is the **common amplifier**; salinisation is the **development-induced exception**.
- ✅ **Khullar's seven implications:** sand drift onto fertile land · excessive erosion by wind and *"to some extent"* water · sand in water bodies · lowered water table · more wasteland · less production · more frequent and intense droughts.
- 📰 ✅ **CA-6:** **COP16, Riyadh, 2-13 December 2024**; theme *"Our Land. Our Future."*; one of the three **Rio Conventions**; **197 Parties — 196 countries and the European Union**; **30th anniversary**; largest UN land conference to date; first in the Middle East and North Africa region. 📰 ✅ **CA-1:** India hosted **COP14, September 2019**; **LDN and restoration of 26 million ha by 2030**.
- ❗ **The dryland boundary is a convention.** ❗ No aridity-index threshold is stated anywhere in this file.

## G. Restoration, mapwork and the owned answer (L16-L18)

- ❗ **The principle:** a measure is **matched or mismatched to a pathway at a place**. ❗ Never list measures pathway-blind.
- ✅ **Khullar's five measures:** intensive tree plantation · **CAZRI mulch squares** · controlled grazing with **new pastures** · ban on indiscriminate felling · **alternative fuels**. ✅ *"Shifting sand dunes in Bikaner, Barmer, Churu, Jaisalmer and Jhunjhunun districts of Rajasthan cover an approximate area of 74 thousand sq km. Central Arid Zone Research Institute, Jodhpur has suggested mulching them… Mulches are put in small squares and serve as an effective physical barrier to the moving sand."* ❗ The Institute's own site **could not be reached**; **no CAZRI publication is quoted**.
- ⚠️ **Mechanisms supplied from Arc A:** plantation → L3's rungs · mulch squares → **L4 saltation interruption** · grazing control → **L9 pavement lag** plus L3 cover · felling ban and alternative fuels → vegetative, demand-side.
- ❗ **The canal trade-off:** ✅ *"Although Indira Gandhi canal in Rajasthan has turned the sandy desert into a granary, it has given birth to serious problems of salinity and alkalinity."* ✅ Majid: **682 km**. ❗ **Irrigation relocates stored salt upward; it does not import it.** ❗ **Drainage with leaching is the only strongly matched chemical-column measure.**
- 📰 ✅ **Aravalli Green Wall (CA-3):** **5 km buffer**; **Haryana, Rajasthan, Gujarat, Delhi**; landscape *"span over 6 million hectares"*; **native species on scrubland, wasteland and degraded forest land**; **pond, lake and stream rejuvenation**; **agroforestry and pasture development**; the **National Action Plan to Combat Desertification and Land Degradation Through Forestry Interventions** and an **ICFRE agroforestry FAQ** unveiled the same day. ❗ **[N11] The circulating "1,400 km" figure is excluded** — absent from the first-party release. ❗ **No outcome is claimed.**
- ❗ **[U11] Three instruments, never summed:** **Bonn Challenge** — Paris 2015, **13 mha by 2020 + 8 mha by 2030 = 21 mha**, *"increased to 26 mha"* at **COP14, 2019**, reporting assigned to **IUCN-India** · **Land Degradation Neutrality** — *"restoration of 26 Million ha… by 2030"* · **Twenty Point Programme** afforestation — *"approximately 18.94 million hectares during the period 2011-12 to 2021-22."* ❗ No fetched document reconciles them.
- 📰 ✅ **National Forest Policy, 1988:** *"a minimum of one-third of the total land area under forest or tree cover."* ✅ Khullar's antecedent: **20-25 %** raised to **33 %** in the second five year plan, **20 % plains / 60 % hills**. ❗ A **cover norm is not a degradation measurement**.
- ✅ **Mid-century framing, attributed:** **S.P. Chatterjee** — *"soil erosion is the greatest single evil to Indian agriculture and animal husbandry"*; **S.I. Kayastha** — *"with soil conservation people rise and with its destruction they fall"*; **G.T. Renner** — *"the greatest good to the largest number for the longest time"*; *"about two thirds of our arable land needs conservation measures."* 🔁 **Crop rotation, strip cropping, contour ploughing and terracing are named and handed off.**
- ✅ **Mapwork placements with their distinguishing lines:** **Thar** *"Monsoon-margin hot desert"*, ❗ *"Great Rann is a salt marsh"* · **Aravalli** SW-NE, *"800 km from Delhi to Ahmedabad"*, ❗ not east-west · **Luni** to the Rann, ❗ *"Does not normally reach the Arabian Sea"* · **Sambhar** 65 km west of Jaipur, ❗ *"Not a coastal lagoon"* · **Rann of Kutch** ❗ *"Not a conventional sand desert"* · **Sir Creek** ❗ *"a tidal estuary, not a river boundary throughout"* · **Sabarmati** ❗ *"Not sourced in the Western Ghats"* · **Desert National Park** Jaisalmer-Barmer, ❗ *"Not the Rann of Kutch"*, **a location only** · **Hemis** Ladakh cold desert, ❗ *"Not in Himachal Pradesh"* · **Khetri** copper against **Zawar** zinc-lead.
- ❗ **No map-bank row exists** for the **Indira Gandhi Canal**, **Marusthali**, **Rajasthan Bagar**, **Barmer** or **Jaisalmer**; each is placed from **Khullar folios 76-77 and 211** with the page cited.
- ❗ **Two lines are not features:** the **25 cm isohyet** and the dashed **"LIMIT OF WIND EROSION"**.
- ✅ **Confusable pairs:** **Sahara / Sahel** · **Namib / Kalahari** · Gobi / Taklamakan · Atacama / Patagonian · **Thar / Great Rann** · **Desert National Park / Rann of Kutch**.
- 📰 ✅ **CA-4, 21 Dec 2025:** Aravallis across **37 districts**; the uniform **"100 metres above local relief"** criterion adopted by all States, from Rajasthan's **2002 Committee Report** on the **Richard Murphy** classification, in force since **09.01.2006**; the range = landforms *"within 500 metres of two adjoining Hills of height 100 metre or more"*; **no mining within 1.0 km**; Committee per order of **9 May 2024**, further directions **12 Aug 2025**, with **Delhi, Haryana, Rajasthan, Gujarat** forest secretaries plus **FSI, CEC, GSI**. ❗ Nothing after that date is asserted.
- ✅ **The owned demand:** **2026 GS-I Q6** — *"Discuss the role of aeolian processes in desertification and land degradation."* **10 marks / 150 words.** ✅ Route: *"Deflation, abrasion, saltation, dust transport and deposition; human amplification; land-productivity effects and controls."* ✅ CA role: *"Policy examples only."* ✅ Readiness **FULL**.
- ❗ **The six-block spine:** **[A]** frame 20 · **[B]** chain 45 · **[C]** productivity 25 · **[D]** human with caveat 25 · **[E]** matched controls 20 · **[F]** limit with anchor 15. 🔑 **F-M-P-H-C-L.** ❗ **Write [A] first, [F] last, and never cut [F].**
- ❗ **Three failure modes:** the landform catalogue · the synonym collapse · the unbounded claim. ❗ Plus importing the advancing-desert image.
- ❗ **Three precision ceilings, stated:** **no aeolian share** of India's degraded area · **no rate** of degradation · **no recovery timescale** for any measure.

# COVERAGE MATRIX

## Part 1 — The eight content strands against the eighteen lessons

⚠️ The eight strands are those the generation brief names: **aridity controls; wind erosion, transport and deposition; desert landforms; drainage and soils; Thar origin and evolution; desertification drivers and indicators; land restoration; India mapwork and verified previous-year demands.** ❗ Every strand is delivered, and every lesson serves at least one.

| Strand | Primary lessons | Supporting lessons | Decisive content delivered |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1. Aridity controls** | **L1**, **L10** | L2, L9, L15 | The water-balance definition; the five independent routes with their signature locations and their limits; the multi-cause rule; the **[N5]** correction to the latitude band; the Thar's four partial controls and the moisture-present-rain-absent formulation |
| **2. Wind erosion, transport and deposition** | **L4**, **L5**, **L6**, **L7** | L3, L9, L14 | Attrition, deflation and abrasion separated; fluid against impact threshold; the three sorted modes with grain sizes, trajectories, distances and jobs; the self-amplifying saltation cascade; height-zoned abrasion; dune form as a two-variable readout; loess as proof of export |
| **3. Desert landforms** | **L2**, **L5**, **L6**, **L8** | L7, L9 | Five surface types with named type-areas; the full erosional and depositional inventories; the four mandatory drawings; **pediment against bajada**; **playa against the wider desert** |
| **4. Drainage and soils** | **L8**, **L9**, **L12** | L2, L14, L16 | The arid flood regime and the closed-basin profile; the salt-trap argument by arithmetic; the Aridisol profile with its accumulation sequence; desert pavement as a **self-limiting feedback**; the Luni, the saline-lake cluster and the Rann correction |
| **5. Thar origin and evolution** | **L10**, **L11**, **L13** | L2, L12, L17 | The monsoon-margin reading; the four-figure area spread **[N4]**; the full west-to-east mosaic with its six local terms and four gradients; the **[N2]** open question; the **[N1]** advancing-desert correction; the **[N13]** Aravalli transition; the determinism specimen |
| **6. Desertification drivers and indicators** | **L14**, **L15** | L3, L9, L12, L13 | The **[N3]** contradiction ledger; the four-part test for a hectare figure; the **[N9]** reconciliation; the **[N12]** rounding; Tables 7.1, 7.2 and 7.3; the five-term separation; the four pathways; the mapped-condition-against-trend control |
| **7. Land restoration** | **L16** | L9, L13, L15, L18 | The process-to-measure matching grid; the five book measures with mechanisms supplied; the canal trade-off; **[N11]** the excluded corridor figure; **[U11]** the three unreconciled instruments; the conservation-agronomy hand-off |
| **8. India mapwork and verified demands** | **L17**, **L18** | L10, L11, L12, L14 | The national-scale sketch map with map-bank corroboration on every row; the world deserts that earn a place; the confusable pairs; the five figures; the owned demand with its six-block spine; the seven routed demands by year and number |

## Part 5 — Visual grammar register

## Scope, boundary and typing

- A **Mains-focused** edition of Geography Topic 07, integrating the world/general Basic owner with its India-applied Advanced companion in **one file, one lesson sequence, eighteen lessons**. ❗ Arc A (L1-L9) is world process; Arc B (L10-L17) is the Thar and India application; **L18** is the synthesis capstone. ❗ **Every Arc-B lesson carries at least one explicit back-reference to an Arc-A mechanism by lesson number**, and L18 references lessons from both arcs.
- **Ownership stated precisely.** ⚠️ This topic owns **arid landforms and the Thar degradation application**. 🔁 Topic 04 owns weathering and groundwater foundations; Topic 05 owns general fluvial process; Topic 18 owns hot- and mid-latitude desert climate; Topic 09 owns lakes; Topic 25 owns cold deserts and poles; the environment-and-ecology file owns the desertification convention, the policy demand and the protected-area network.
- ❗ **A syllabus-mapping gap is reported, not amended.** The repository's official syllabus mapping file lists **neither** `basic/07` **nor** `advanced/07` under any clause, although several neighbouring physical-geography files appear under the world-physical-geography clause. ⚠️ This edition therefore claims the **demand families it substantively serves** — *"Salient features of world's physical geography"* for Arc A and *"geographical features and their location-changes… and the effects of such changes"* for Arc B — and cites the subject master framework's arid-geomorphology line and its anti-determinism line, under which this topic is **first named**, as its in-repository mapping authority. ❗ **Amending the mapping file is a separate, separately-authorised task and was not performed.**
- **Typing convention.** ✅ marks a statement carried in a named source — a canonical owner, a book page listed below, or an official instrument. ⚠️ marks analytical inference, evaluation, standard synthesis or a flagged limitation. 📰 marks a dated current or institutional item. 🔁 marks an ownership routing or a cross-lesson reference. ❗ marks a hard prohibition or a discipline an examiner can check. ❌ marks an error being corrected. ❓ marks a question this edition declines to settle. 🇮🇳 marks an India-specific application. 🔑 marks a memory aid.

## Page conventions, recorded so no citation here is mistaken for another edition's pagination

- **Majid Husain, *Indian and World Geography*** uses **chapter-relative folios** of the form `<chapter>.<page>`. It is the **one text-searchable local volume**, and its citations take the form **printed p.X.YZ (local scan PDF p.N)**.
- **G.C. Leong, *Certificate Physical and Human Geography***, carries **one printed page per scan page, rotated**, with a **non-uniform printed-to-scan offset book-wide**. ❗ It is therefore cited by **local-scan PDF page**, with a printed folio given only where directly visible on the render.
- **D.R. Khullar, *India: A Comprehensive Geography***, carries **two printed folios per scan page, stacked and rotated**, with an offset that **drifts across the book**. ❗ Folios are **read off each render and never computed**, and citations give both the local-scan PDF page and the folio.
- **Majid Husain, *Geography of India***, is image-only. ❗ It is cited once, by printed folio and local-scan PDF page, and **only as a determinism specimen**.

| **CA-2** | Press Information Bureau, same Ministry — *"Land Degradation and Desertification"* | **07 August 2023, 4:11 PM**, Release ID **1946401** | *"the land degradation and desertification in the country has been estimated to be 97.84 million hectares in 2018-19"*; an **online portal** developed with the Space Applications Centre *"for visualization of degraded area of land with the processes causing degradation"*; a **Centre of Excellence** envisaged at **ICFRE Dehradun** for South-South cooperation; **IUCN-India** assigned Bonn Challenge reporting; the Bonn Challenge sequence of **13 plus 8 equals 21 mha, increased to 26 mha at COP14 in 2019**; **Twenty Point Programme** afforestation of *"approximately 18.94 million hectares during the period 2011-12 to 2021-22"*; the **National Forest Policy, 1988** one-third goal |
| **CA-3** | Press Information Bureau, same Ministry — *"Shri Bhupender Yadav launches Aravalli Green Wall Project…"* | **25 March 2023, 5:33 PM**, Release ID **1910745** | A **5 km buffer** around the Aravalli Hill Range in **Haryana, Rajasthan, Gujarat and Delhi**, *"where the Aravalli hills landscape span over 6 million hectares of land"*; **native species on scrubland, wasteland and degraded forest land**; **rejuvenation of ponds, lakes and streams**; **agroforestry and pasture development**; the objective *"To prevent eastward expansion of Thar Desert and to reduce land degradation by creating green barriers that will prevent soil erosion, desertification and dust storms"*; the **National Action Plan to Combat Desertification and Land Degradation Through Forestry Interventions** and an **ICFRE agroforestry FAQ** unveiled the same day |
| **CA-4** | Press Information Bureau Research Unit backgrounder — *"Aravalli Hills: Protecting Ecology and Ensuring Sustainable Development"* | **21 December 2025**, 7 pages | The Supreme Court *"emphasized the ecological importance of the Aravalli range as a barrier against desertification, a groundwater recharge zone, and a biodiversity habitat"*; Aravallis recognised **across 37 districts**; the Committee constituted per order of **9 May 2024** with further directions of **12 August 2025**, comprising forest secretaries of **Delhi, Haryana, Rajasthan and Gujarat** with the **Forest Survey of India**, the **Central Empowered Committee** and the **Geological Survey of India**; only Rajasthan previously had a formal mining-regulation definition, on its **2002 Committee Report** applying the **Richard Murphy landform classification**, in force since **09.01.2006**; all States agreed to the uniform **"100 metres above local relief"** criterion; the range explained as *"all the landforms which exists within 500 metres of two adjoining Hills of height 100 metre or more"*; **no mining within 1.0 km** |
| **CA-5** | Space Applications Centre, Indian Space Research Organisation — the VEDAS **Desertification Status Mapping Atlas index** | Fetched during the preparation of this edition; ❗ **the page is undated on its face** | The atlas is a **series**: a Land Degradation Vulnerability Atlas; the ***Desertification and Land Degradation Atlas of India 2021***; *Desertification Land Degradation Atlas 2018* Volumes 1 and 2; a **Desertification Status Mapping Technical Guideline**; and a *Desertification and Land Degradation Atlas of India 2016*; with a separate dashboard listed under applications. ❗ The **2021** edition is named explicitly rather than described as "the latest" |
| **CA-6** | United Nations Convention to Combat Desertification — the sixteenth Conference of the Parties page | **COP16, 2 to 13 December 2024** | COP16 took place in **Riyadh, Saudi Arabia**; theme ***"Our Land. Our Future."***; the Convention is *"one of the three major UN treaties known as the Rio Conventions, alongside climate and biodiversity"*; the Conference is *"the main decision-making body of UNCCD's 197 Parties — 196 countries and the European Union"*; it coincided with the **thirtieth anniversary**, was *"the largest UN land conference to date"* and was *"the first UNCCD COP held in the Middle East and North Africa region"* |

❗ **Eleven of the eighteen lessons — L1 to L9, L11 and L12 — declare in their pre-teach checklist that no first-party current-affairs anchor was obtained for their mechanism.** ⚠️ That is a true statement about world-process and book-physiography content, and **no anchor has been borrowed from a neighbouring lesson or invented** to disguise it.

## Residual risks accepted and how each is mitigated

| Risk | Mitigation in this edition |
|---|---|
| **Gazetteer drift** — the books supply dozens of named deserts, dunes, depressions and districts | ❗ The rosters are excluded. **L2** and **L17** keep only features carrying a cause, a demand hook or a map-bank row |
| **Policy drift** — three of seven routed demands sit with the environment-and-ecology owner | ❗ The agency test is applied to every paragraph. **L15** and **L16** each carry a **visible hand-off line**, and all seven adjacent demands are listed by year and number |
| **Encroachment on the desert-climate owner** — the closest boundary in the lane | ❗ The regime is stated in **one line** at L1 and L10 and handed off. ❗ **Zero bustard content anywhere** |
| **Determinism** — three seductive claims are present in the sources | ❗ **[N1]**, **[N2]** and **[N13]** are explicit correction or open-question boxes, and the **refuse-single-cause argument map** is a master map |
| **Stale-statistic leakage** | ❗ **[N1]**, **[N3]**, **[N4]** and **[N9]** each require an explicit correction; every area, percentage, rate, tonnage, height and district figure carries its issuing agency or book, its boundary or method where stated, and its period |
| **Scale mixing** | ❗ Six named scales, **one per figure**, with the scale **stated on every drawing** |
| **Visual monotony** | ❗ Eighteen distinct primary grammars; ❗ **exactly one pure table**, at L14, where the content is an audit |
| **India-example gaps in the world-process lessons** | ❗ Where India lacks the feature at scale — the great erg, the loess plateau, the inselberg province, the bajada and pediment — ❗ **the absence is named and a Thar analogue substituted with its page citation**, rather than skipped or faked |
| **Over-length model answers** | ❗ Every model is **word-counted, not estimated**; lesson-local models are at or under 150 words and final 15- and 20-mark models at or under 250 |
| **Conflating the three restoration instruments** | ❗ **[U11]**; the commitment-against-outcome rule is applied at L15, L16 and L18 |
